# Wave 06 — Windows rebuild engine: offline hives, bcdboot, DISM, native VHDX target — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A whole-machine Windows snapshot (UEFI/GPT, NTFS `C:\`, captured under VSS by the Windows agent) rebuilds on a Windows host into a bootable disk — a native dynamic VHDX on a Hyper-V host now (Restore-as-VM, DR rehearsal, proven end-to-end on KIT in this wave) and a physical disk from WinPE once W07 ships the media (built and CI-gated here, not claimed as shipped) — through the SAME `rebuild.Run` phase loop, resume state, `Result` shape and server state machine the Linux engine uses, with registry hives applied offline (never into the live registry), `MountedDevices` rewritten so the restored volume is `C:` on any disk, the EFI system partition regenerated by `bcdboot`, boot-critical storage drivers forced to boot-start, operator-supplied driver packages injected with DISM, NTFS security descriptors captured and restored (so `SAM` never comes back world-readable), and every unsupported situation refused before the target is touched.

**Architecture:** Four PRs on one wave issue (#5499), in this order. **W06a (agent fidelity + guards, "Part of #5499") — ships first, in the next agent release, because snapshots taken before it can never carry security descriptors:** NTFS security descriptors captured for every file AND every directory (deduplicated SD table in the manifest) and re-applied on every Windows restore; `layout.Assess` hardened for Windows sources (dynamic disks, Storage Spaces, ReFS, missing root partition GUID); the two prerequisite fixes (#6631 item 2 `pathClean`, validate's path key); Windows layout capture gains disk GUID + GPT attributes. **W06b (agent engine core, "Part of #5499"):** per-platform phase table selected from `layout.Platform` + `runtime.GOOS`, a Windows-shaped `WinSystem` seam (Win32 IOCTL partitioning preserving partition GUIDs, `format.com`, folder mount points + a temporary ESP letter, virtdisk.dll VHDX create/attach with non-permanent lifetime, `bcdboot`/`dism` runners) with a cross-platform fake, Windows preflight/provision/restore-tree/validate, resume, startup cleanup of leftovers, and a real-VHDX Windows CI gate. **W06c (agent OS state, "Part of #5499"):** offline hive loading via `RegLoadKeyW`, the Windows `RestoreSystemStateOffline` (all-or-nothing hive fallback, `Select\Default` control set, boot-start storage edits, `MountedDevices` rewrite), `boot` (bcdboot + DISM), `identity` (marker / `-restored` computer name / enrollment strip / MachineGuid / DC refusal), `encryption` (post-restore intent file; re-enable executes in W07), validate's ESP + BCD-hive checks. **W06d (API + web + docs + lab, "Closes #5499"):** `bare_metal_recoveries.platform` column, platform-matched rebuild hosts, drive-letter-absolute paths in the command/DR/VM-restore schemas, per-host-OS DR output dir, optional same-host `New-VM` (no NIC unless a switch is named) after a Windows VHDX rebuild, wizard/DR host pickers filtered by snapshot platform, i18n, docs, follow-up issues, and the KIT lab proof (WIN-A whole-machine snapshot → VHDX → VM boots; host NVRAM unchanged).

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.26 (`agent/internal/backup/{rebuild,layout,systemstate,bmr}`, `agent/internal/securefs`, `agent/cmd/breeze-backup`), `golang.org/x/sys/windows` v0.48 (+ `windows/registry`) and `NewLazySystemDLL` procs for `virtdisk.dll` (`CreateVirtualDisk`, `AttachVirtualDisk`, `DetachVirtualDisk`, `GetVirtualDiskPhysicalPath`) and `advapi32.dll` (`RegLoadKeyW`, `RegUnLoadKeyW`, `GetFileSecurityW`, `SetFileSecurityW`) — CGO stays off; Windows inbox tools `format.com`, `bcdboot.exe`, `dism.exe`; Hono + zod + Drizzle + Vitest (`apps/api`); PostgreSQL migration; React + Vitest (`apps/web`); GitHub `windows-latest` runner for the VHDX gate; KIT Hyper-V lab.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/backup/2026-09-10-bare-metal-boot-media-recovery-design.md` §2 (#2 file backup + system state + layout, #4 no secrets on media, #5 layouts, #6 identity, #7 one engine), §5.1–5.3 (Windows whole-machine root `C:\` under VSS, layout.json Windows fields, guard), §6 (phases; "Windows applies hives, BCD, drivers, certs, firewall offline"; "`bcdboot <root>\Windows /s <efi> /f UEFI`, DISM driver injection"; "BitLocker: enable on first boot via a one-shot task"; targets `disk`/`vhdx`), §8.3 (Restore-as-VM/DR call the engine with a `vhdx` target; rehearsal forces `identity:new`), §9 (nothing written before preflight; rehearsals never resume the production identity), §10 ("VHDX on Windows in wave 6"), §11 wave 6. Design record for the hive question: `docs/superpowers/plans/backup/2026-09-10-bmr-windows-offline-hive-decision.md` (Option B stays the contract for reinstall-then-recover; boot-media mode applies offline — this wave). Not §7.2/§7.3 Windows media builder + console (W07), not §8.4 (W08). Design review: Fable position + independent Opus adversarial review 2026-09-22 (Codex quorum unavailable until 2026-09-26 — recorded, not skipped). **Deliberate deviations from the spec** are in §4 below and in the self-review.

**Depends on:** W03 (engine), W04a (recovery codes/tokens/marker), W05 (`bare_metal_rebuild` command, DR step, VM-restore rebuild route), W09 (capability negotiation, admission). Independent of W07/W08. Related open issues NOT solved here: #6664 (fixed 4 h helper timeout — a Windows system disk of ~130k+ files will hit it), #5470 (Option B cleanup of the reinstall-then-recover restorer), #3019 (Windows CI package coverage in general), #6446 (sparse holes), #5582 (case-only twins), #6489/#6490 (token-mode provider edge cases). #6631 item 2 IS fixed here (Part A Task 1).

## Global Constraints

- **One engine, one `Run`, one `Result`.** `rebuild.Run(ctx, Options) (*Result, error)` keeps its signature; `AllPhases` stays the same eight phases in the same order for every platform (`convert` is recorded `skipped` on Windows: the VHDX is written natively, there is no raw staging file). `runState`, `PhaseResult`, `RefusalError`, `ObjectAdmission`, bounded reporting and `bmr.PostRecoveryProgress` are unchanged in shape; `runState` gains `Platform`, `HostOS` and `Volumes`.
- **Platform table, not a second engine.** Phase functions come from `platformPhases[layout.Platform]` chosen once in `Run` after the layout is fetched (`resolvePlatform`, Part B Task 8). `layout.Platform != hostPlatform()` is a **refusal** — `snapshot platform %q cannot be rebuilt on a %s host` — recorded as a refused `preflight` row; never a fallback. A resume whose state file records a different `Platform`/`HostOS` is refused (`resume state was written on a %s host for platform %q`). `NewSystem()` keeps returning nil off Linux; `NewWinSystem()` returns nil off Windows; the table decides which seam is required.
- **Two seams, never widened into each other.** Linux phases use `System` (unchanged). Windows phases use `WinSystem` (`agent/internal/backup/rebuild/winsystem.go`, untagged interface; `winsystem_windows.go` real; `winsystem_fake_test.go` fake). `Options.WinSystem` sits next to `Options.System`.
- **No PowerShell, no diskpart in the engine.** Partition tables: `FSCTL_LOCK_VOLUME` + `FSCTL_DISMOUNT_VOLUME` on every existing volume of the target disk, `IOCTL_DISK_DELETE_DRIVE_LAYOUT`, zero the first and last MiB, `IOCTL_DISK_CREATE_DISK` (GPT; disk GUID from the layout when recorded), `IOCTL_DISK_SET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX` (partition GUIDs and GPT attributes preserved), `IOCTL_DISK_UPDATE_PROPERTIES`. Filesystems: `format.com`. Mounts: root/recovery on **folder mount points** under the staging root (`SetVolumeMountPointW`), the ESP on a **temporary drive letter** for `bcdboot /s` (documented as a volume letter). VHDX: `CreateVirtualDisk` (dynamic, 32 MiB block, logical sector size = source disk's) + `AttachVirtualDisk` with `ATTACH_VIRTUAL_DISK_FLAG_NO_DRIVE_LETTER` and **no** `PERMANENT_LIFETIME` (the handle stays open for the run; a helper crash auto-detaches). Hives: `RegLoadKeyW`/`RegUnLoadKeyW` under `SeBackupPrivilege` + `SeRestorePrivilege` enabled explicitly with `AdjustTokenPrivileges` (SYSTEM holds them disabled). Every external tool goes through `WinSystem.Run(ctx, name, args...)` so the fake records it.
- **No automount surprises.** Every partition is written with GPT attribute `0x8000000000000000` (no drive letter) during the run; `winValidate`'s last layout write clears it on root/data partitions (Recovery keeps `0x8000000000000001`). The engine never depends on a host-assigned letter.
- **Partition GUIDs are identity, and `MountedDevices` is rewritten regardless.** Provision writes each partition with its recorded `PartUUID`. The offline SYSTEM edit (Part C Task 14) ALWAYS writes `MountedDevices\\DosDevices\C:` = `DMIO:ID:` + the restored root partition's 16-byte GUID (mixed-endian GUID bytes, exactly what `IOCTL_DISK_GET_PARTITION_INFO_EX` returns) and deletes every `\DosDevices\<X>:` value whose `DMIO:ID` GUID is not on the rebuilt disk; `\??\Volume{…}` values are left alone. A Windows snapshot whose root partition has no `PartUUID` is refused by `Assess` (W06a) with `root partition has no recorded partition GUID; back up again with a current agent`.
- **Hives: file tree first, all-or-nothing artifact fallback, never the live registry.** The VSS file tree (`Windows\System32\config\{SYSTEM,SOFTWARE,SAM,SECURITY,DEFAULT}` + `.LOG1/.LOG2`, `Users\*\NTUSER.DAT`) is authoritative. If ANY of SYSTEM/SOFTWARE/SAM/SECURITY is missing from the tree, ALL FOUR are replaced from `system-state/registry/<HIVE>` and their `.LOG1/.LOG2` deleted (mixing capture points splits LSA secrets from the machine password); if any artifact is missing too → error `hive %s missing from both the file tree and the system-state artifacts`. Nothing in this wave ever calls `reg restore`, `reg import`, `bcdedit /import` or touches the host's own `HKLM`. Hive mounts are run-scoped (`HKLM\BRZ_<runid>_<HIVE>`), every key handle is closed and `RegFlushKey` called before `RegUnLoadKeyW`, and `Run` starts by unloading any leftover `HKLM\BRZ_*` mounts and detaching any VHDX it recorded in a stale state file (Part B Task 12).
- **Control set.** Edits go to `ControlSet00<Select\Default>`; if `Select\Current` differs, the same edits are applied to it too.
- **Refuse before destructive work.** Every refusal this wave introduces fires in `preflight` (before `provision`). `disk:` targets on Windows are refused unless the process runs in WinPE (`HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\MiniNT` exists); a live Windows host may only write `vhdx:` targets. A `disk:` target that is the system disk, the WinPE media disk, read-only, offline, or holds a `Windows\System32\config\SYSTEM` on any volume is refused (the last one overridable by `Options.ForceDisk`, CLI `--force-disk` only). A source with `Services\NTDS` in its SYSTEM hive (domain controller) is refused unless `Options.AllowDomainController` (CLI `--allow-domain-controller` only; never from the server payload) — this check runs in preflight against the `system-state/registry/SYSTEM` artifact when present, else in the restore phase before any hive edit.
- **Sizing.** Hard links become copies (WinSxS ↔ System32), so the root partition's minimum and the VHDX free-space check use `max(manifest logical bytes × 1.1, layout UsedBytes × 1.1)`; `PlanPartitionsWindows` therefore takes the manifest total, and the Windows preflight fetches the manifest BEFORE planning. The root partition (`C:`) grows; partitions after it (Recovery) keep their size.
- **Paths.** On Windows the restore keys every file by `journalEntryKey(f)` (`OriginalPath` first) with the volume stripped (`stripVolumeAndLeadingSeparators`, `restore.go:546`); validate MUST use the same key (Part A Task 3 fixes `validate.go:61`). The token-mode `pathClean` (`bmr/download_provider.go:681`) must use `path.Clean` on the forward-slash key, never `filepath.Clean` (#6631 item 2).
- **Work dir.** `Options.WorkRoot` (new): Windows `vhdx:` → `%ProgramData%\Breeze\rebuild\work\<snapshotID>` on the HOST (a dynamic VHDX never shrinks); Windows `disk:` (WinPE) → `<root mount>\$breeze-rebuild-work` (the `X:` RAM drive is ≤ 512 MB), removed before validate; Linux unchanged. `StateDir` defaults to `%ProgramData%\Breeze\rebuild` on Windows.
- **Identity.** `original`: marker JSON at `<root>\ProgramData\Breeze\data\recovery-marker.json` (what `heartbeat.LoadRecoveryMarker(config.GetDataDir())` reads on Windows). `new`: computer name `<BASE>-RESTORED` upper-cased with `BASE` truncated so the total ≤ 15 (NetBIOS), written to `Control\ComputerName\ComputerName\ComputerName`, `Control\ComputerName\ActiveComputerName\ComputerName` (if present) and `Services\Tcpip\Parameters\{Hostname,NV Hostname}` in the chosen control set(s); a name already ending in `-RESTORED` is unchanged; new `SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Cryptography\MachineGuid`; `secrets.yaml` deleted; enrollment keys stripped from `agent.yaml` (shared `stripEnrollment`). Machine SID is NOT changed.
- **Encryption.** A BitLocker source is restored PLAINTEXT. The phase writes `<root>\ProgramData\Breeze\data\post-restore-actions.json` (`{"schemaVersion":1,"bitlocker":{"reencrypt":true,"volume":"C:"},"winre":{"enable":false,"reason":"recovery partition contents were not backed up"}}`) on `disk:` targets and records the warning `volume C: was BitLocker-protected at backup; it is restored unencrypted; re-encryption on first boot arrives with the Windows recovery media (W07)`. `vhdx:` (rehearsal) → skipped `rehearsal image left unencrypted`. No `RunOnce` (it runs at first interactive logon under that user's token, not before logon). The agent-side executor for the intent file is W07's.
- **Drivers.** Offline SYSTEM edits for the fixed list `storahci, stornvme, storflt, vmbus, storvsc, pciide, intelide, iaStorV, iaStorVD, iaStorAC, iaStorAVC` — only keys that EXIST get `Start=0` and their `StartOverride` subkey deleted. DISM `/Image:<root> /Add-Driver /Driver:<dir> /Recurse` runs once per `Options.DriverDirs` entry (CLI `--drivers <dir>`, repeatable); a DISM failure fails the run. No dirs → warning `no driver packages supplied; inbox drivers only`. The Hyper-V injector in `hyperv/vmrestore.go:334` is neither reused nor touched. W07 contract: WinPE build ≥ the newest guest build it must service.
- **ESP and boot.** `bcdboot <root>\Windows /s <ESP letter>: /f UEFI /v` — preferring `<root>\Windows\System32\bcdboot.exe` (campaign §11 "Lab additions") and falling back to PATH with a warning. For `vhdx:` targets on a live host add `/p` (preserve the host's existing UEFI firmware entries) and the lab proof asserts `bcdedit /enum firmware` is unchanged before/after. `system-state/boot/bcd_export` is never imported. Validate loads `<ESP>\EFI\Microsoft\Boot\BCD` as a hive and asserts `Objects\{9dea862c-5cdd-4e70-acc1-f32b344d4795}\Elements\23000003` exists (the boot manager's default entry) — no localised `bcdedit` text parsing. The Recovery partition is recreated and formatted but left empty; warning `Windows RE partition contents were not backed up`; no `reagentc` call (the `Winre.wim` is absent).
- **NTFS security descriptors (W06a).** Capture on Windows for every file AND every directory (the walker records a `KindDir` entry for every directory when SD capture is on): `GetFileSecurityW(path, OWNER|GROUP|DACL [|SACL when SeSecurityPrivilege enables])` → self-relative SD bytes → manifest table `securityDescriptors []string` (base64, deduplicated by sha256) + per-entry `sdIndex` (1-based; 0/omitted = unknown). Apply on Windows restores only: files immediately after content (`SetFileSecurityW`, never `SetNamedSecurityInfoW` — no inheritance propagation, no child walk), directories in a post-pass after every file is placed, both under `SeRestorePrivilege` + `SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege` + `SeSecurityPrivilege`; failures are warnings, not failed files. Manifests without the table restore exactly as today. Hard links, ADS, EFS, creation time and junction reparse data remain out of scope (documented; `BackupRead`/`BackupWrite` streams noted as the future path).
- **Server contract (W06d).** `bare_metal_recoveries` gains `platform text NULL CHECK (platform IN ('linux','windows'))`, set at create from `layout_manifest.platform`, registered in `CORE_TENANT_EXPORT_POLICY` as `included`; migration named to sort after the newest committed file (check `ls apps/api/migrations | tail -1` at implementation time; slot `2026-10-28-110000-bare-metal-recoveries-platform.sql` as of 2026-09-23; newest committed was `2026-10-28-100000-metric-anomaly-episodes.sql`). Host rule: `devices.osType` must equal the snapshot platform or the route returns 409 `rebuild_host_unsupported` with `details: {osType, snapshotPlatform}`. Absolute-path refinements accept `^[A-Za-z]:\\` (no UNC) or `^/` via one shared helper `isAbsoluteRebuildPath` in a NEW pool-free leaf module `apps/api/src/services/bareMetalRebuildSchemas.ts` (also home of `hypervOptionsSchema`; imports only `zod`), imported by `bareMetalRebuildCommand.ts`, `routes/backup/schemas.ts` and `drBareMetalRebuildStep.ts` — `schemas.ts` must stay pool-free at import time (`bareMetalRebuildCommand.ts` → `commandQueue` → live `db`), which is why the helper cannot live there (default `outputDir` becomes host-OS-dependent at DISPATCH: `C:\ProgramData\Breeze\rebuild\out` for Windows; the VHDX name is joined with the host's separator). The payload gains optional `hyperv: {vmName, switchName?, memoryMb?, cpuCount?}` (Windows hosts only, else 400 `hyperv is only valid for Windows rebuild hosts`); the helper creates a Gen2 VM after `validate` with **no network adapter unless `switchName` is set**, through a new `hyperv.runPSContext` with a 10-minute timeout, and reports `vmCreated: true`.
- **Registries.** New column → export-policy entry (above). No new tables, env vars or workers. Windows CI package list (`ci.yml:1874`): Part A adds `./internal/backup/wingpt ./internal/backup/layout`, Part B adds `./internal/backup/rebuild ./internal/backup/bmr ./internal/backup/winhive` — no duplicates. Must-not-skip steps (pattern `:1909-1923`): Part A = 4 PASS (security-descriptor tests in `internal/backup` via `-run`), Part B = 1 PASS (`TestRun_WindowsVhdxRealSystem`, `BREEZE_REBUILD_VHDX_TEST=1`), Part C = 1 PASS (real offline-hive test). `.github/scripts/qemu-gate-paths.txt` gains `agent/internal/backup/wingpt/` (A) and `agent/internal/backup/winhive/` (B) — `TestQEMUGatePathsMatchBackupDependencySet` fails otherwise. Test seam: `rebuild.SetHostPlatformForTest(func() string) func()` (Part B) — `rebuild`'s `TestMain` pins `hostPlatform` to `linux` so the existing Linux-engine suite stays green on macOS and on the Windows CI job; `cmd/breeze-backup/rebuild_host_test.go` does the same for the CLI tests.
- **Test commands:** agent `cd agent && go test -race ./internal/backup/... ./cmd/breeze-backup/...`; cross-compile `cd agent && GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 go vet ./internal/backup/... ./cmd/breeze-backup/... && GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 go test -c -o /dev/null ./internal/backup/rebuild`; agent lint `golangci-lint run --new-from-rev=origin/main ./...`; API unit `cd apps/api && npx vitest run <path>`; API integration (export policy, RLS) `cd apps/api && DATABASE_URL=… npx vitest run -c vitest.integration.config.ts <path>`; web `cd apps/web && npx vitest run <path>`; API typecheck `cd apps/api && NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=14336 npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json; echo exit=$?` (never pipe tsc to tail). Red first, every task.
- **No git in workers.** The controller commits after each task passes with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. No internal hostnames/IPs in code, tests or docs (lab names WIN-A/KIT are fine; addresses are not).

## Review Focus

Inputs the spec implies but no existing test exercises; each line's test is pinned to the owning task.

1. **A Windows snapshot on a Linux host, or a Linux snapshot on a Windows host** — refused before any manifest download or write with the platform reason (Part B Task 8 `TestRun_RefusesPlatformHostMismatch`).
2. **A Windows snapshot captured WITHOUT VSS** (`OriginalPath` empty, `SourcePath = C:\…`) — restore, validate and the hive lookup key on the same stripped path (Part A Task 3 `TestValidate_UsesRestoreKey`, Part C Task 14 `TestHiveSource_TreeWithoutOriginalPath`).
3. **`C:` is not the last partition** (Windows 11 default: ESP, MSR, C:, Recovery) — `C:` grows, Recovery stays fixed after it, and the minimum comes from the manifest total, not `UsedBytes` (Part B Task 9 `TestPlanWindows_GrowsRootBeforeRecovery`, `TestPlanWindows_MinimumFromManifestBytes`).
4. **A restored SYSTEM hive whose `MountedDevices` maps `C:` to a partition GUID that is not on the rebuilt disk** — the value is rewritten and stale letters removed (Part C Task 14 `TestMountedDevices_RewritesRootAndDropsStaleLetters`).
5. **`identity:new` with a 12-character computer name, and with a name already ending in `-RESTORED`** — 15-char truncation, no double suffix (Part C Task 14 `TestNewComputerName_Truncates15`, `TestNewComputerName_NoDoubleSuffix` — the pure `winhive.NewComputerName`).

## 0. Ground truth (verified 2026-09-22 on main @ `33b29159e6`)

**Engine (`agent/internal/backup/rebuild/`, untagged package; the `run` struct gains `winSys, platform, diskNumber, winAttached, volumes, espVolume, espDir, rootDir, recoveryDir, hives, controlSets` in Parts B/C).** `Run` (`engine.go:79-168`) validates options, defaults `StateDir` to `/var/lib/breeze/rebuild` (`:91`), `StagingRoot` to `<StateDir>/mnt/<id>` (`:94`), calls `NewSystem()` when `Options.System == nil` and returns `ErrUnsupportedHost` if nil (`:96-101`); phase table literal `:115-119`; resume marks completed non-preflight/validate phases skipped and calls `r.reattach` for provision/restore (`:121-131`); refusals → `Status "refused"` (`:138-145`); on completion the state file and `restoreWorkRoot(StateDir)` are removed (`:161-164`). `run` struct `:34-69` (`disk`, `detach`, `staging`, `rootMount`, `treeMounts`, `mounts`, `stateStaging`, `releaseErr`, `layout`, `manifest`, `warnings`, `failedFiles`). `runState` `:21-31`. `teardown` `:243-281`. `System` interface `system.go:18-49`; `system_other.go` returns nil for `!linux`. `preflight` (`preflight.go:56`): layout fetch/schema `:58-66`, `Assess` `:67`, **`lay.Platform != "linux"` refusal `:70-72`**, disk in-use/root checks `:78-95`, image/vhdx sizing `:96-128` (`qemu-img` + 1.5× free space), `PlanPartitions` `:133`, `fetchManifest` `:142`, `ObjectAdmission` sweep `:149-167`, `bmr.DownloadSystemState` `:175-196`. `PlanPartitions` (`plan.go:54`): `supportedFilesystems` `:11`, `validateFSUUID` `:29`, grow index `:70-74`, MSR skipped `:79`. `provision` (`provision.go:166`), `runWithBusyRetry` `:92`, `attach` `:258`, `reattach` `:280`. `mountTree` (`restore_tree.go:18`, requires `/` `:32`); `restoreTree` `:56` → `backup.RestoreFromSnapshotContext(ctx, provider, RestoreConfig{SnapshotID, TargetPath: r.staging, WorkRoot: restoreWorkRoot(StateDir)}, …)` `:71`, `ensureMountpoints` `:105`, `bmr.RestoreSystemStateOffline(ctx, r.staging, r.stateStaging)` `:117`. `boot` (`boot.go:106`). `identity` (`identity.go:22`): marker `:29-37`; `applyNewIdentity` `:48`; `stripEnrollment(agentYAML)` `:65` (keep the signature); `encryption` `:91`. `validate` (`validate.go:32`): `identityMutatedPaths` `:20`, **sample keyed by `f.SourcePath` `:61`**, GRUB/EFI artefacts `:72-79`, fstab `:82-95`, `Sync` + `teardown` `:97-101`. `convert` (`convert.go:15`). Types (`types.go`): `Target` `:31`, `RawPath()` `:42`, `Options` `:137-175`, `Result` `:178-209`, `ObjectAdmission` `:332`, `RefusalError` `:338`. Fakes: `fakeSystem` (`system_fake_test.go`), `memProvider`/`seedSnapshot`/`testLayout` (`engine_test.go:140-176`), `bmr.SetRunCommandForTest` in `TestMain` (`engine_test.go:42-49`).

**Layout (`agent/internal/backup/layout/`).** Types `types.go:60-110`; GUID constants `:48-57`; roles `:29-38`; `isRootMount` accepts `C:\` `:148`. Windows collector `collect_windows.go` (`runCommand` var `:12`, script `:24-38`: `Get-Disk` selects `Number,FriendlyName,SerialNumber,Size,PartitionStyle,IsSystem,IsBoot,LogicalSectorSize,BusType` — NOT `Guid`; `Get-Partition` selects `…,Guid,GptType,…` — NOT the GPT attributes); parser `windows_parse.go:87-218` (`PartUUID = normalizeGUID(p.Guid)` `:161`, `FSUUID` = volume GUID `:196`, `Encryption = bitlocker` `:200`, roles `:203-215`). `Assess` (`guard.go:44`): `supportedRootFS = ext4/xfs/ntfs` `:30`, `systemMountPoints` POSIX only `:36-38`, feature scan Linux-only (`:84-99`: lvm/luks/raid/btrfs/zfs) — no Windows dynamic-disk, Storage Spaces or ReFS checks; BitLocker not refused.

**File backup/restore on Windows (`agent/internal/backup/`).** `SnapshotFile` (`snapshot.go:251-325`); `journalEntryKey` `:360`; `Snapshot` `:166-196` (`ID, Timestamp, Files, Size, FormatVersion, BaseSnapshotID, BackupIdentity`); `FormatVersion = 2` at `:664`, `manifestFormatFidelity` at `:1228`, `snapshotNeedsFidelityFormat` `:331`. Walker: `dirNeedsEntry` (`backup.go:1527-1533`: Windows records only EMPTY dirs), file entry construction `:1709-1788`, `winFileAttrs` (`winattrs_windows.go:18`). `RestoreConfig{SnapshotID, TargetPath, SelectedPaths, WorkRoot}` (`restore.go:22`); `stripVolumeAndLeadingSeparators` `:546`; content install via `securefs.InstallFileWithAttrs(base, relative, source, mode, modTime, owner, winAttrs)` (`securefs/path.go:53`, called `restore.go:292`); `applyEntryMetadata(targetPath, entry, applyOwnership) []string` `:790` (chmod → owner → chtimes → `applyWinAttrs` LAST); the `KindDir` pass `:352-388` (`securefs.InstallDir` + `applyWinAttrs`); `RestoreContentlessEntry` `:823`. `owner_windows.go` no-op. Whole-machine Windows excludes `apps/web/src/components/configurationPolicies/featureTabs/backupTabPresets.ts:232-242`.

**System state (`agent/internal/backup/systemstate/`).** Windows collector `state_windows.go`: required `registry`, `boot` (`:25-28`); hives via `reg save` → `registry/<HIVE>` named `registry_<HIVE>` (`:125-134`, `helpers.go:359-402`; SECURITY often blocked by Defender — O13); `boot/bcd_export`; `drivers/inventory.csv`; `services/services.txt`; `firewall/rules.wfw`; `certs/*`; `tasks/tasks.csv`; `features/features.txt`. `bmr.DownloadSystemState(ctx, provider, snapshotID, expect, stagingDir) (*SystemStateManifest, []string, error)` (`bmr/download_system_state.go:42`) writes `<stagingDir>/<artifact.Path>`. `bmr.RestoreSystemStateOffline(ctx, root, stagingDir) ([]string, error)`: Linux `restore_linux.go:665`; **`restore_offline_other.go` (`!linux`) returns "offline system-state apply is Linux-only in this release"**. `bmr/restore_windows.go` (live restorer) untouched (#5470). BitLocker escrow exists: `agent/internal/security/recoverykeys.go` → `device_recovery_keys` (`apps/api/src/db/schema/recoveryKeys.ts:13`).

## 1. Engine contract additions

```go
// types.go — additions
type Options struct {
	// … existing fields unchanged …
	WinSystem             WinSystem // nil → NewWinSystem() (real on windows, nil elsewhere)
	WorkRoot              string    // restore scratch (see Global Constraints "Work dir"); "" → platform default
	DriverDirs            []string  // DISM /Add-Driver sources (Windows boot phase); empty = inbox only
	ForceDisk             bool      // Windows disk targets: overwrite a disk holding a Windows installation (CLI only)
	AllowDomainController bool      // Windows: allow a source whose SYSTEM hive has Services\NTDS (CLI only)
}

type Result struct {
	// … existing fields unchanged …
	Platform  string `json:"platform"`            // "linux" | "windows" (from the layout)
	VMCreated bool   `json:"vmCreated,omitempty"` // set by the helper after HyperV creation succeeded
}

// runState additions (engine.go)
//   Platform string            `json:"platform"`
//   HostOS   string            `json:"hostOs"`
//   Volumes  map[int]string    `json:"volumes,omitempty"` // partition number → \\?\Volume{...}\ (Windows)
```

```go
// engine.go — platform table (replaces the literal slice at :115-119)
type phaseFn struct {
	phase Phase
	fn    func(context.Context, *run) error
}

var platformPhases = map[string][]phaseFn{
	"linux": {
		{PhasePreflight, preflight}, {PhaseProvision, provision}, {PhaseRestore, restoreTree},
		{PhaseBoot, boot}, {PhaseIdentity, identity}, {PhaseEncryption, encryption}, {PhaseValidate, validate},
		{PhaseConvert, convert},
	},
	"windows": {
		{PhasePreflight, winPreflight}, {PhaseProvision, winProvision}, {PhaseRestore, winRestoreTree},
		{PhaseBoot, winBoot}, {PhaseIdentity, winIdentity}, {PhaseEncryption, winEncryption}, {PhaseValidate, winValidate},
		{PhaseConvert, winConvert},
	},
}

// hostPlatform maps runtime.GOOS onto layout.Platform values; "" = no engine.
var hostPlatform = func() string {
	switch runtime.GOOS {
	case "linux", "windows":
		return runtime.GOOS
	}
	return ""
}
```

`Run` order: validate options → per-host defaults (`StateDir`, `StagingRoot`, `WorkRoot`) → `cleanupLeftovers(r)` (Windows: unload `HKLM\BRZ_*`, detach a VHDX named in a stale state file for this target) → `loadState` → `resolvePlatform(ctx, r)` = layout fetch/schema check moved out of `preflight.go:58-66` (both platforms) + platform/host refusal + resume platform check → seam check (`linux` needs `System`, `windows` needs `WinSystem`; nil → `ErrUnsupportedHost`, message `the rebuild engine has no implementation for this host platform`) → loop over `platformPhases[plat]`. The Linux `preflight` keeps working with `r.layout` already set (it must no longer refetch). `r.result.Platform = plat`.

```go
// winsystem.go — the Windows seam (untagged interface)
type WinSystem interface {
	Run(ctx context.Context, name string, args ...string) ([]byte, error)
	LookPath(name string) (string, error)
	InWinPE() bool                                   // HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\MiniNT exists
	SystemDiskNumber() (int, error)                  // disk holding %SystemRoot%'s volume; -1 when %SystemRoot% is a RAM disk (WinPE)
	MediaDiskNumbers() ([]int, error)                // WinPE: disks holding the boot media (BootDevice / \\?\Volume of X:'s source); empty elsewhere
	DiskInfo(diskNumber int) (WinDiskInfo, error)    // IOCTL_DISK_GET_LENGTH_INFO, IOCTL_STORAGE_QUERY_PROPERTY (access alignment), IOCTL_DISK_GET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES
	VolumesOnDisk(diskNumber int) ([]WinVolume, error) // FindFirstVolumeW walk + IOCTL_STORAGE_GET_DEVICE_NUMBER
	CreateVHDX(path string, sizeBytes int64, logicalSectorSize int) error
	AttachVHDX(path string) (diskNumber int, detach func() error, err error) // NO_DRIVE_LETTER, non-permanent; detach closes the handle
	WipeDisk(ctx context.Context, diskNumber int) error // lock+dismount every volume, IOCTL_DISK_DELETE_DRIVE_LAYOUT, zero first/last MiB
	WriteGPT(diskNumber int, diskGUID string, parts []WinGPTPartition) error // CREATE_DISK + SET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX + UPDATE_PROPERTIES
	ReadGPT(diskNumber int) (diskGUID string, parts []WinGPTPartition, err error) // IOCTL_DISK_GET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX (validate's assertion + the attribute clear)
	WaitForVolumes(ctx context.Context, diskNumber int, want int) ([]WinVolume, error)
	Format(ctx context.Context, volumeGUIDPath, filesystem, label string) error // format.com <vol> /FS:<NTFS|FAT32> /Q /Y [/V:<label>]
	MountVolume(volumeGUIDPath, dir string) error    // MkdirAll(dir) + SetVolumeMountPointW(dir+`\`, volumeGUIDPath)
	UnmountVolume(dir string) error                  // DeleteVolumeMountPointW(dir+`\`)
	AssignLetter(volumeGUIDPath string) (letter string, release func() error, err error) // first free letter Z..D via SetVolumeMountPointW
	FlushVolume(volumeGUIDPath string) error         // FlushFileBuffers on the volume handle
	FreeSpace(dir string) (int64, error)             // GetDiskFreeSpaceExW
	LoadHive(hiveFile, mountName string) (winhive.Handle, error) // winhive.Load: RegLoadKeyW(HKLM, mountName, hiveFile) with SeBackup/SeRestore enabled
	UnloadStaleHives(prefix string) (int, error)     // RegUnLoadKeyW every HKLM subkey starting with prefix
	HasWindowsTree(volumeGUIDPath string) (bool, error) // <vol>\Windows\System32\config\SYSTEM exists
	SetPartitionAttributes(diskNumber, number int, attrs uint64) error // wingpt.SetPartitionAttributes (validate clears the no-letter bit)
	DetachVHDXByPath(path string) (detached bool, err error)         // cleanupLeftovers: OpenVirtualDisk + DetachVirtualDisk; false when nothing was attached
}

type WinDiskInfo struct {
	SizeBytes         int64
	LogicalSectorSize int
	ReadOnly, Offline bool
}

type WinVolume struct {
	GUIDPath        string // \\?\Volume{...}\
	DiskNumber      int
	PartitionNumber int
	DriveLetter     string // "" when none
}

type WinGPTPartition struct {
	Number     int
	TypeGUID   string // lower-case, no braces
	PartGUID   string // preserved from layout; "" → new
	Name       string // ≤ 36 UTF-16 code units
	StartBytes int64
	SizeBytes  int64
	Attributes uint64 // 0x1 platform-required; 0x8000000000000000 no drive letter
}

// winhive.Handle is one loaded hive; Close flushes and unloads it. (Lives in
// package winhive — see "Package placement" — because bmr and rebuild both
// use it and bmr can never import rebuild.)
type Handle interface {
	Root() Key // the mounted HKLM\<mountName>
	Close() error
}

// winhive.Key is the minimal registry surface the offline edits use, so the
// edit logic is testable off Windows against winhive.Fake (Part C).
type Key interface {
	OpenKey(path string) (Key, error) // path relative, `\`-separated; winhive.ErrNotExist when absent
	CreateKey(path string) (Key, error)
	DeleteKey(path string) error          // recursive; nil when absent
	GetString(name string) (string, error)
	GetDWORD(name string) (uint32, error)
	GetBinary(name string) ([]byte, error)
	SetString(name, value string) error
	SetDWORD(name string, value uint32) error
	SetBinary(name string, value []byte) error
	DeleteValue(name string) error         // nil when absent
	ValueNames() ([]string, error)
	SubKeyNames() ([]string, error)
	Close() error
}
```

**Package placement (import-cycle rule: `rebuild` imports `bmr`, so `bmr` can never import `rebuild`).**
- `agent/internal/backup/wingpt` (NEW, W06a): the GPT layout codec + IOCTL reader/writer. Untagged: `type Partition struct{Number int; TypeGUID, PartGUID, Name string; StartBytes, SizeBytes int64; Attributes uint64}`, `type Layout struct{DiskGUID string; SectorSize int; Partitions []Partition}`, `func EncodeDriveLayoutEx(l Layout) ([]byte, error)`, `func DecodeDriveLayoutEx(b []byte) (Layout, error)`, `func GUIDToBytes(s string) ([16]byte, error)` / `func BytesToGUID(b [16]byte) string` (Windows mixed-endian; `winhive` carries its own 30-line copy of the GUID codec, pinned by the same test vector, so it imports nothing first-party), `func NewGUID() (string, error)`, `func EncodeCreateDiskGPT(diskGUID string) ([]byte, error)`, `func CopyUsableRange(dst, current []byte) error`, `func CtlCode(deviceType, function, method, access uint32) uint32` with the pinned package vars (Go names `IOCTLDiskGetDriveLayoutEx` etc., values as the C names) `IOCTL_DISK_GET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX = 0x00070050`, `IOCTL_DISK_SET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX = 0x0007C054`, `IOCTL_DISK_CREATE_DISK = 0x0007C058`, `IOCTL_DISK_DELETE_DRIVE_LAYOUT = 0x0007C100`, `IOCTL_DISK_UPDATE_PROPERTIES = 0x00070140`, `IOCTL_DISK_GET_LENGTH_INFO = 0x0007405C`, `IOCTL_STORAGE_GET_DEVICE_NUMBER = 0x002D1080`, `IOCTL_DISK_GET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES = 0x000700F0`, `FSCTL_LOCK_VOLUME = 0x00090018`, `FSCTL_DISMOUNT_VOLUME = 0x00090020` (each pinned by a `CtlCode` test). Windows-tagged: `func ReadLayout(diskNumber int) (Layout, error)`, `func WriteLayout(diskNumber int, l Layout) error`, `func CreateDisk(diskNumber int, diskGUID string) error`, `func DeleteLayout(diskNumber int) error`, `func UpdateProperties(diskNumber int) error`, `func SetPartitionAttributes(diskNumber, number int, attrs uint64) error`. The layout collector (W06a Task 2) uses `ReadLayout` to fill `Disk.GUID` and `Partition.Attributes`; `WinSystem` (W06b Task 12) wraps the writers.
- `agent/internal/backup/winhive` (NEW, W06c, but its interface is declared in W06b Task 7 so `WinSystem.LoadHive` compiles): untagged `type Key interface{…}` (the `hiveKey` surface below, exported as `Key`), `type Handle interface{ Root() Key; Close() error }`, `type Fake` (in-memory tree, exported so both `rebuild` and `bmr` tests can seed it), and the pure edit functions (`ControlSets`, `RewriteMountedDevices`, `ForceBootStartStorage`, `SetComputerName`, `NewMachineGuid`, `HasNTDS`, `DefaultBCDEntryExists`); windows-tagged `func Load(hiveFile, mountName string) (Handle, error)` (RegLoadKeyW under the privileges) and `func UnloadStale(prefix string) (int, error)`. Both `bmr` (Windows `RestoreSystemStateOffline`) and `rebuild` (identity/encryption/validate) import it.
- `layout.Disk` gains `GUID string \`json:"guid,omitempty"\`` and `layout.Partition` gains `Attributes uint64 \`json:"attributes,omitempty"\`` (W06a Task 2). `SchemaVersion` stays 1 (additive, omitempty).

The fake (`winsystem_fake_test.go`) mirrors `fakeSystem`: records every `Run`; `WriteGPT` "creates" one temp dir per non-MSR partition and synthesises `\\?\Volume{<partGUID>}\`; `MountVolume` maps that volume to `dir` by creating `dir` as a real directory so restored files land in the temp tree; `AssignLetter` returns `"Z"` and records it; `LoadHive` returns a `fakeHive` (in-memory `hiveKey` tree pre-seeded by the test); `InWinPE`, `SystemDiskNumber`, `MediaDiskNumbers`, `DiskInfo`, `freeSpace`, `fail map[string]error` are fields.

## 2. Windows phase semantics

| Phase | Does | Refuses / warns (exact strings) |
|---|---|---|
| `winPreflight` | `layout.Assess` (already Windows-hardened by W06a); `fetchManifest` FIRST (sizing needs it); `PlanPartitionsWindows(src, targetSize, sector, manifestBytes)`; target: `disk:` → `InWinPE()` (or `ForceDisk`), not `SystemDiskNumber()`, not in `MediaDiskNumbers()`, `DiskInfo` not read-only/offline, no volume `HasWindowsTree` (or `ForceDisk`), size from `DiskInfo`; `vhdx:` → size = `ImageSizeBytes` or source disk size, `FreeSpace(filepath.Dir(Path)) ≥ minimum`; admission sweep + system-state download via the shared `preflightVerify(ctx, r)` extracted from `preflight.go:142-196`; DC check against the staged `registry/SYSTEM` artifact when present (`hasNTDS`) | `disk targets are only supported from Breeze recovery media (WinPE); use a vhdx target on a running Windows host`; `target disk %d holds the running system`; `target disk %d holds the recovery media`; `target disk %d is read-only or offline`; `target disk %d contains a Windows installation; pass --force-disk to overwrite it`; `partition %d filesystem %q is not supported by the Windows engine (ntfs, fat32)`; `not enough free space for the VHDX: need %d, have %d`; `target is too small: …` (shared wording); `source is a domain controller (Services\NTDS present); pass --allow-domain-controller and read the DC recovery guidance` |
| `winProvision` | `attach` (disk number, or `CreateVHDX` + `AttachVHDX`); `WipeDisk`; `WriteGPT` with recorded GUIDs, names, attributes (all partitions `| 0x8000000000000000`; Recovery `| 0x1`; ESP/MSR `| 0x1`); `WaitForVolumes(want = non-MSR count)`; `Format` each (ESP `FAT32`, others `NTFS`, recorded label); `runState.Volumes[number] = GUIDPath`; `saveState` | busy retry via `runWithBusyRetry` semantics (a `winRunWithRetry` twin on `WinSystem.Run`, same 5×500 ms) |
| `winRestoreTree` | mount root at `<staging>\root`, Recovery at `<staging>\recovery` (folder mount points), remember the ESP volume for boot; `RestoreFromSnapshotContext` with `TargetPath = <staging>\root`, `WorkRoot` per Global Constraints; then the `applyWindowsSystemState(ctx, r)` hook (Part B stages it as a hard error unless `SkipBoot`; Part C Task 14 makes it call `bmr.RestoreSystemStateOfflineWindows(ctx, rootDir, stateStaging, rootPartGUID, onDiskGUIDs, runID, allowDC, r.winSys.LoadHive)` and keep the returned hives in `r.hives`) with `StateApplied` semantics exactly as Linux; `bmr/restore_offline_other.go` stays `!linux` and the Windows engine never calls the generic `RestoreSystemStateOffline` | partial restore handling identical to Linux |
| `winBoot` | `AssignLetter(esp)`; `bcdboot <root>\Windows /s <L>: /f UEFI /v [/p]` with the restored tree's `bcdboot.exe` when present; ensure `<L>:\EFI\Boot\bootx64.efi` (copy `EFI\Microsoft\Boot\bootmgfw.efi` if absent); DISM per `DriverDirs`; release the letter | `no driver packages supplied; inbox drivers only`; `host bcdboot used (restored tree has no bcdboot.exe)` |
| `winIdentity` | marker / `-RESTORED` / MachineGuid / strip via `hiveKey` on the loaded SYSTEM+SOFTWARE hives (loaded once by the restore phase's state apply and kept in `run.hives` until validate closes them — so identity and encryption edit the same mounted hives) | `no recovery marker given; the server will not auto-complete this recovery` |
| `winEncryption` | intent file on `disk:`; skipped on `vhdx:` | warnings per Global Constraints |
| `winValidate` | `ExpectSystemState` gate; `validateOSState(r)` hook (Part B: no-op; Part C Task 17: close hives FIRST, ESP files, BCD hive check); checksum sample keyed by `restoreKey(f)` skipping `Windows\System32\config\*`, `ProgramData\Breeze\*`; ESP files exist; BCD hive check; `ReadGPT` asserts partition GUIDs + clears the no-letter attribute on root/data via `WriteGPT`-less `SetGPTAttributes` (add to seam: `SetPartitionAttributes(diskNumber, number int, attrs uint64) error`); remove work dir (disk targets); `FlushVolume` each; unmount; release; detach | `ESP is missing %s after boot phase`; `BCD store has no default boot entry`; `partition %d GUID changed during the run (%s → %s)` |
| `winConvert` | `recordSkipped(PhaseConvert, "vhdx written natively; no conversion")` | — |

## 3. Refusal / outcome matrix (acceptance criteria — every row has a test)

| # | Situation | Where | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Windows snapshot, Linux host | `resolvePlatform` | refused `snapshot platform "windows" cannot be rebuilt on a linux host`; no manifest download, no write |
| R2 | Linux snapshot, Windows host | same | refused (mirror) |
| R3 | darwin host | same | `ErrUnsupportedHost` |
| R4 | resume state from another host OS/platform | `resolvePlatform` | refused `resume state was written on a %s host for platform %q` |
| R5 | `disk:` target, not WinPE, no `ForceDisk` | winPreflight | refused (exact string §2) |
| R6 | `disk:` = system disk / media disk / read-only / offline | winPreflight | refused (exact strings §2) |
| R7 | `disk:` holds a Windows tree, no `ForceDisk` | winPreflight | refused |
| R8 | Windows layout: root without `PartUUID`; dynamic disk (`5808c8aa-7e8f-42e0-85d2-e1e90434cfb3` / `af9b60a0-1431-4f62-bc68-3311714a69ad`); Storage Spaces (`e75caf8f-f680-4cee-afa3-b001e56efc2d`); ReFS root | `layout.Assess` (W06a) | `Restorable=false` with reasons `root partition has no recorded partition GUID; back up again with a current agent`, `dynamic (LDM) disks are not supported`, `Storage Spaces are not supported`, `root filesystem type "refs" is not supported (ext4, xfs, ntfs)` |
| R9 | partition filesystem `exfat` | winPreflight | refused `…not supported by the Windows engine (ntfs, fat32)` |
| R10 | `vhdx:` free space < minimum | winPreflight | refused `not enough free space for the VHDX…` |
| R11 | manifest total × 1.1 > layout used × 1.1 | `PlanPartitionsWindows` | minimum uses the manifest total |
| R12 | `vhdx:` on Windows | winPreflight/convert | no `qemu-img` requirement; convert skipped; no `.raw` file |
| R13 | token provider does not admit a key | shared `preflightVerify` | refused exactly as Linux |
| R14 | `ExpectSystemState` and no manifest | shared | refused exactly as Linux |
| R15 | DC source without `AllowDomainController` | winPreflight (artifact) or restore (tree) | refused / failed before any hive edit |
| R16 | tree lacks one of the 4 hives, artifacts have all 4 | state apply | all 4 replaced + logs deleted; warning `registry hives restored from the system-state artifacts, not the file tree`; StateApplied true |
| R17 | tree lacks a hive, an artifact missing too | state apply | error `hive %s missing from both the file tree and the system-state artifacts` |
| R18 | `MountedDevices\\DosDevices\C:` ≠ root GUID; `\DosDevices\E:` → GUID not on disk | state apply | `C:` rewritten, `E:` deleted, `\??\Volume{…}` untouched; warning `MountedDevices: C: remapped to the restored partition; 1 stale drive letter removed` |
| R19 | `Select\Default` ≠ `Select\Current` | state apply | both control sets edited |
| R20 | `storahci` key with `StartOverride`, `stornvme` absent | state apply | `storahci Start=0`, `StartOverride` deleted; `stornvme` not created |
| R21 | `identity:new`, name `FILESERVER01` | winIdentity | `FILESE-RESTORED` in all four values; `MachineGuid` changed |
| R22 | `identity:new`, name `SRV-RESTORED` | winIdentity | unchanged |
| R23 | BitLocker root + `disk:` | winEncryption | intent file written; warning recorded |
| R24 | BitLocker root + `vhdx:` | winEncryption | skipped `rehearsal image left unencrypted` |
| R25 | `DriverDirs` empty / set + dism fails | winBoot | warning / run fails with dism output |
| R26 | restored tree has no `bcdboot.exe` | winBoot | host bcdboot, warning |
| R27 | `vhdx:` target | winBoot | bcdboot args contain `/p`; `disk:` args do not |
| R28 | ESP missing `bootmgfw.efi`; BCD lacks `23000003` | winValidate | error strings §2 |
| R29 | resume after a failure in `boot` | `Run` | provision + restore skipped, volumes remounted from `runState.Volumes`, VHDX re-attached by path |
| R30 | stale `HKLM\BRZ_*` mount or attached VHDX from a crashed run | `cleanupLeftovers` | unloaded/detached before preflight; warning recorded |
| R31 | API: host OS ≠ snapshot platform | VM-restore route, DR dispatch | 409 `rebuild_host_unsupported` `{osType, snapshotPlatform}` / DR `failedDispatches` `rebuild_host_unsupported` |
| R32 | API: `outputPath` `C:\images\x.vhdx` / `\\server\share\x.vhdx` / `x.vhdx` | schemas | accepted / rejected / rejected |
| R33 | API: DR `outputDir` omitted, Windows host | dispatch | `C:\ProgramData\Breeze\rebuild\out\<deviceId>-<recoveryId>.vhdx` |
| R34 | API: `hyperv` block on a Linux host | route | 400 `hyperv is only valid for Windows rebuild hosts` |
| R35 | helper: `hyperv` block, no `switchName` | helper | VM created with no network adapter; `vmCreated: true` |
| R36 | `bare_metal_recoveries.platform` | create | `'windows'` for a Windows layout; export policy includes it |
| R37 | manifest with `securityDescriptors` restored on Linux | restore | table ignored |
| R38 | manifest without `sdIndex` restored on Windows | restore | no SD applied (today's behaviour) |
| R39 | SD apply fails for one file | restore | warning; file counted restored |
| R40 | Windows walker with SD capture on | backup | every directory has a `KindDir` entry with `sdIndex`; identical SDs share one table slot |
| R41 | Windows CI VHDX gate skips | CI | job fails (must-not-skip) |

## 4. Deliberate non-goals and deviations (say so in the PR)

- **D-BL (spec deviation):** BitLocker re-enable is NOT executed in this wave — the phase records intent (`post-restore-actions.json`) and W07 executes it from the restored agent service at boot with a NEW recovery password (never "with the escrowed key": carrying it on media contradicts §2 #4). Disk targets cannot be exercised before W07 ships WinPE, so the executor would be untestable here.
- **D-ACL (spec deviation, §12):** NTFS security descriptors ARE captured and restored (W06a). Without them a rebuilt `C:` gives Authenticated Users Modify on `System32\config` (the HiveNightmare class, CVE-2021-36934) and breaks `WindowsApps`. Not droppable. Hard links, ADS, EFS, creation time and junction reparse data stay out of scope (documented).
- **No SID regeneration / sysprep.** `identity:new` clones the machine SID. Domain-joined `new` machines have a broken secure channel (warning); a same-host VM from an `original`-identity image must not get a NIC by default (it would renew the live machine's password).
- **WinRE contents are not restored**; `reagentc` is not run (`Winre.wim` is absent).
- **No captured driver packages.** `driverquery` is inventory only; a capture-side `pnputil /export-driver` is a follow-up.
- **Linux guests on Windows hosts, cross-host `New-VM`, Instant Boot for Linux guests** stay deferred (W05's promise) — follow-up issue in Part D Task 24.
- **#5470 / `restore_windows.go`** untouched. **#6664** untouched.
- **Disk targets are built and CI-tested, not shipped**: the wave's shipped surface is "Windows snapshot → native VHDX on a Windows host" (Restore-as-VM, DR rehearsal, CLI `vhdx:`). W07 owns the disk-target proof.
- **W07 contract from this wave:** `breeze-backup.exe rebuild --target disk:\\.\PhysicalDrive<n> --token … [--drivers X:\breeze\drivers] [--force-disk] [--allow-domain-controller]` inside WinPE with optional components `WinPE-WMI`, `WinPE-SecureStartup`, `WinPE-EnhancedStorage`; `bcdboot.exe`, `dism.exe`, `format.com` are inbox; `X:` is NTFS (folder mount points under `X:\ProgramData\Breeze\rebuild\mnt`); WinPE build ≥ newest guest build; the agent-side executor for `post-restore-actions.json`.

## Part A — W06a (PR 1, "Part of #5499")

### Task 1: `pathClean` uses `path.Clean` on forward-slash keys (#6631 item 2)

**Files:**
- Modify: `agent/internal/backup/bmr/download_provider.go:681-687` (`pathClean`)
- Test: `agent/internal/backup/bmr/download_provider_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces: `pathClean(path string) string` keeps its existing signature and both call sites (`download_provider.go:334`, `:579`) unchanged — only the body changes.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`pathClean` currently does `filepath.ToSlash(filepath.Clean(path))`. `filepath.Clean` is host-OS-aware: on a Windows agent host, `filepath.Clean` treats `\` as the separator and collapses `//` differently than POSIX `path.Clean` does, and — the actual #6631 bug — a key that is already forward-slash-normalized (as every object key this function receives always is: it comes from the manifest's `BackupPath`/journal key, which are always `/`-joined) gets **re-interpreted through host path syntax** before being turned back into a slash string, corrupting a key like `snapshots/a/files/path_0/x.gz` on a Windows host into a different string than the identical Linux-host run produces, because `filepath.Clean` on Windows also collapses a leading `\\` into a UNC-root marker and treats `.`/`..` segment resolution using `\` as well as `/` as separators — two hosts computing `pathClean` over the same logical key can disagree. `path.Clean` (the `path` package, not `path/filepath`) is always POSIX-forward-slash-only regardless of `GOOS`, so it is the only implementation that is deterministic across the fleet.

Add this table test to `agent/internal/backup/bmr/download_provider_test.go` (and `"path"` to that file's import block — the second test below calls `path.Clean`):

```go
func TestPathClean(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name string
		in   string
		want string
	}{
		{
			name: "ordinary object key round-trips",
			in:   "snapshots/a/files/path_0/x.gz",
			want: "snapshots/a/files/path_0/x.gz",
		},
		{
			name: "double slash and dot segment collapse",
			in:   "snapshots/a//files/./x.gz",
			want: "snapshots/a/files/x.gz",
		},
		{
			name: "dot-dot segment resolves within the key",
			in:   "snapshots/a/files/../b/x.gz",
			want: "snapshots/a/b/x.gz",
		},
		{
			name: "empty and dot-only input clean to empty",
			in:   ".",
			want: "",
		},
		{
			name: "trailing slash is dropped like path.Clean",
			in:   "snapshots/a/files/",
			want: "snapshots/a/files",
		},
		{
			// The #6631 case: a `\` inside an object key is a literal byte,
			// not a separator. The old filepath.Clean+ToSlash rewrote it to
			// `/` on a Windows host (red on the Windows CI job, which runs
			// ./internal/backup/bmr from W06b on); path.Clean never does.
			name: "backslash inside a key is preserved",
			in:   `snapshots/a/files/path_0/C:\Users\x.gz`,
			want: `snapshots/a/files/path_0/C:\Users\x.gz`,
		},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got := pathClean(tt.in)
			if got != tt.want {
				t.Errorf("pathClean(%q) = %q, want %q", tt.in, got, tt.want)
			}
			if strings.Count(got, `\`) != strings.Count(tt.in, `\`) {
				t.Errorf("pathClean(%q) = %q changed the key's backslashes; they are literal key bytes on every GOOS", tt.in, got)
			}
		})
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run to verify failure**

`cd agent && go test ./internal/backup/bmr -run TestPathClean -v`

On a Linux/macOS dev host `filepath.Clean` already behaves like `path.Clean` for these forward-slash-only inputs, so **this specific table will not fail natively on this host** — the bug is host-OS-conditional (it is Windows-specific: `filepath.Clean` only diverges from `path.Clean` when `GOOS == "windows"`, because only then does `filepath.Separator` become `\`). To prove the fix is host-OS-independent rather than "happens to already pass on Linux", add a second, explicit cross-host test that drives `pathClean` through Windows semantics directly:

```go
// TestPathClean_MatchesAcrossGOOS proves pathClean's result is independent
// of the host's path.Separator — the actual #6631 bug was that the old
// filepath.Clean-based implementation was NOT independent of it. This
// doesn't (and can't, without GOOS=windows) run filepath.Clean under
// Windows semantics, but it pins the invariant path.Clean already
// guarantees: pathClean must equal the plain path.Clean result for every
// input, on every host, which is only true once filepath.Clean is gone
// entirely — filepath.Clean on this native host is a red herring; grep the
// implementation for "path.Clean" (not "filepath.Clean") to confirm the fix.
func TestPathClean_MatchesAcrossGOOS(t *testing.T) {
	inputs := []string{
		"snapshots/a/files/path_0/x.gz",
		"snapshots/a//files/./x.gz",
		"snapshots/a/files/../b/x.gz",
		"a/b/c",
	}
	for _, in := range inputs {
		want := path.Clean(in)
		if want == "." {
			want = ""
		}
		if got := pathClean(in); got != want {
			t.Errorf("pathClean(%q) = %q, want path.Clean-equivalent %q", in, got, want)
		}
	}
}
```

Before Step 3, confirm by inspection (not by a red run, since the bug doesn't reproduce on a non-Windows CI runner) that the current body imports `path/filepath`, not `path`, and that `TestPathClean_MatchesAcrossGOOS` documents this. Both tests must compile and pass once Step 3 lands; `TestPathClean` alone is enough to catch a regression back to `filepath.Clean` should someone `GOOS=windows` cross-compile-test this package later (Part 0 §"Test commands" cross-compile step: `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 go vet ./internal/backup/...` would not by itself catch a semantic regression, only a compile break — the two tests above are the actual regression guard).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```go
func pathClean(path string) string {
	cleaned := stdpath.Clean(path)
	if cleaned == "." {
		return ""
	}
	return cleaned
}
```

Add the import (aliased `stdpath` to avoid colliding with the existing `path` **parameter** name in `pathClean`'s own signature — the function's argument is itself named `path`, which already shadows the package name `path` if imported unaliased):

```go
import (
	// … existing imports …
	stdpath "path"
)
```

`path/filepath` stays imported — other functions in `download_provider.go` still use it (verified: `go vet` is clean with both imports).

- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests**

```
cd agent && go test -race ./internal/backup/bmr/... -run 'TestPathClean'
cd agent && GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 go vet ./internal/backup/bmr/...
cd agent && go test -race ./internal/backup/bmr/...
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

`git add agent/internal/backup/bmr/download_provider.go agent/internal/backup/bmr/download_provider_test.go && git commit -m "fix(backup): pathClean uses path.Clean, not filepath.Clean (#6631) (W06a)"`

#### Step 1: Write the failing tests

`agent/internal/backup/wingpt/codec_test.go` (new package, no prior code exists — every test here fails with `undefined: wingpt.X` until Step 3):

```go
package wingpt

import (
	"encoding/hex"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// TestCtlCode pins every IOCTL/FSCTL constant this package exports against
// the CTL_CODE macro from <devioctl.h> (deviceType<<16 | access<<14 |
// function<<2 | method), rather than trusting copied hex literals — a wrong
// device-type/function/access combination compiles and links fine and
// silently issues a DIFFERENT ioctl at runtime (the exact failure class the
// vssapi.dll vtable-index comment in agent/internal/backup/vss/vss_windows.go
// warns about for a different API surface).
func TestCtlCode(t *testing.T) {
	const (
		fileDeviceDisk       = 0x00000007
		fileDeviceMassStorage = 0x0000002D
		fileDeviceFileSystem = 0x00000009
		methodBuffered       = 0
		fileAnyAccess        = 0
		fileReadAccess       = 1
		fileReadWriteAccess  = 3
	)
	tests := []struct {
		name                            string
		deviceType, function, method, access uint32
		want                            uint32
	}{
		{"IOCTL_DISK_GET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX", fileDeviceDisk, 0x14, methodBuffered, fileAnyAccess, 0x00070050},
		{"IOCTL_DISK_SET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX", fileDeviceDisk, 0x15, methodBuffered, fileReadWriteAccess, 0x0007C054},
		{"IOCTL_DISK_CREATE_DISK", fileDeviceDisk, 0x16, methodBuffered, fileReadWriteAccess, 0x0007C058},
		{"IOCTL_DISK_DELETE_DRIVE_LAYOUT", fileDeviceDisk, 0x40, methodBuffered, fileReadWriteAccess, 0x0007C100},
		{"IOCTL_DISK_UPDATE_PROPERTIES", fileDeviceDisk, 0x50, methodBuffered, fileAnyAccess, 0x00070140},
		{"IOCTL_DISK_GET_LENGTH_INFO", fileDeviceDisk, 0x17, methodBuffered, fileReadAccess, 0x0007405C},
		{"IOCTL_STORAGE_GET_DEVICE_NUMBER", fileDeviceMassStorage, 0x420, methodBuffered, fileAnyAccess, 0x002D1080},
		{"IOCTL_DISK_GET_DISK_ATTRIBUTES", fileDeviceDisk, 0x3C, methodBuffered, fileAnyAccess, 0x000700F0},
		{"FSCTL_LOCK_VOLUME", fileDeviceFileSystem, 6, methodBuffered, fileAnyAccess, 0x00090018},
		{"FSCTL_DISMOUNT_VOLUME", fileDeviceFileSystem, 8, methodBuffered, fileAnyAccess, 0x00090020},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			if got := CtlCode(tt.deviceType, tt.function, tt.method, tt.access); got != tt.want {
				t.Errorf("CtlCode(%#x,%#x,%#x,%#x) = %#08x, want %#08x", tt.deviceType, tt.function, tt.method, tt.access, got, tt.want)
			}
		})
	}
	// The package's own exported constants must equal the same computation
	// — this is what actually protects the constants from silent hand-edit
	// drift, not the table above by itself.
	if IOCTLDiskGetDriveLayoutEx != 0x00070050 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskGetDriveLayoutEx = %#08x", IOCTLDiskGetDriveLayoutEx)
	}
	if IOCTLDiskSetDriveLayoutEx != 0x0007C054 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskSetDriveLayoutEx = %#08x", IOCTLDiskSetDriveLayoutEx)
	}
	if IOCTLDiskCreateDisk != 0x0007C058 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskCreateDisk = %#08x", IOCTLDiskCreateDisk)
	}
	if IOCTLDiskDeleteDriveLayout != 0x0007C100 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskDeleteDriveLayout = %#08x", IOCTLDiskDeleteDriveLayout)
	}
	if IOCTLDiskUpdateProperties != 0x00070140 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskUpdateProperties = %#08x", IOCTLDiskUpdateProperties)
	}
	if IOCTLDiskGetLengthInfo != 0x0007405C {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskGetLengthInfo = %#08x", IOCTLDiskGetLengthInfo)
	}
	if IOCTLStorageGetDeviceNumber != 0x002D1080 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLStorageGetDeviceNumber = %#08x", IOCTLStorageGetDeviceNumber)
	}
	if IOCTLDiskGetDiskAttributes != 0x000700F0 {
		t.Errorf("IOCTLDiskGetDiskAttributes = %#08x", IOCTLDiskGetDiskAttributes)
	}
	if FSCTLLockVolume != 0x00090018 {
		t.Errorf("FSCTLLockVolume = %#08x", FSCTLLockVolume)
	}
	if FSCTLDismountVolume != 0x00090020 {
		t.Errorf("FSCTLDismountVolume = %#08x", FSCTLDismountVolume)
	}
}

// TestGUIDToBytesKnownVector pins the mixed-endian encoding against the
// EFI System Partition type GUID's well-known byte representation, exactly
// as it appears on the wire in a live PARTITION_INFORMATION_GPT.PartitionType
// field on any Windows host: 28 73 2A C1 1F F8 D2 11 BA 4B 00 A0 C9 3E C9 3B.
func TestGUIDToBytesKnownVector(t *testing.T) {
	const want = "28732ac11ff8d211ba4b00a0c93ec93b"
	got, err := GUIDToBytes("c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if hex.EncodeToString(got[:]) != want {
		t.Errorf("GUIDToBytes = %x, want %s", got, want)
	}
}

func TestGUIDBytesRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	guids := []string{
		"c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b",
		"e3c9e316-0b5c-4db8-817d-f92df00215ae",
		"00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000",
		"ffffffff-ffff-ffff-ffff-ffffffffffff",
	}
	for _, g := range guids {
		b, err := GUIDToBytes(g)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("GUIDToBytes(%s): %v", g, err)
		}
		if got := BytesToGUID(b); got != g {
			t.Errorf("BytesToGUID(GUIDToBytes(%s)) = %s, want %s", g, got, g)
		}
	}
}

func TestGUIDToBytesRejectsMalformed(t *testing.T) {
	for _, bad := range []string{"", "not-a-guid", "c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b", "{c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93bXX}"} {
		if _, err := GUIDToBytes(bad); err == nil {
			t.Errorf("GUIDToBytes(%q) = nil error, want error", bad)
		}
	}
}

// TestEncodeDecodeDriveLayoutExRoundTrip and TestEncodeDriveLayoutExByteOffsets
// pin the exact byte layout of DRIVE_LAYOUT_INFORMATION_EX / PARTITION_INFORMATION_EX
// on x64: header 48 bytes (PartitionStyle u32@0, PartitionCount u32@4, GPT.DiskId
// GUID@8, StartingUsableOffset i64@24, UsableLength i64@32, MaxPartitionCount
// u32@40, 4 bytes pad@44), then one 144-byte PARTITION_INFORMATION_EX per
// partition: PartitionStyle u32@0, StartingOffset i64@8, PartitionLength i64@16,
// PartitionNumber u32@24, RewritePartition u8@28, IsServicePartition u8@29,
// GPT.PartitionType GUID@32, GPT.PartitionId GUID@48, GPT.Attributes u64@64,
// GPT.Name [36]u16@72.
func TestEncodeDecodeDriveLayoutExRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	l := Layout{
		DiskGUID: "11111111-2222-3333-4444-555555555555",
		Partitions: []Partition{
			{
				Number:     1,
				TypeGUID:   "c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b",
				PartGUID:   "aaaaaaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddd-eeeeeeeeeeee",
				Name:       "EFI system partition",
				StartBytes: 1048576,
				SizeBytes:  104857600,
				Attributes: 0x0000000000000001, // GPT_ATTRIBUTE_PLATFORM_REQUIRED
			},
			{
				Number:     2,
				TypeGUID:   "ebd0a0a2-b9e5-4433-87c0-68b6b72699c7",
				PartGUID:   "12121212-3434-5656-7878-909090909090",
				Name:       "Basic data partition",
				StartBytes: 105906176,
				SizeBytes:  136700000000,
				Attributes: 0x8000000000000000, // GPT_BASIC_DATA_ATTRIBUTE_NO_DRIVE_LETTER
			},
		},
	}
	encoded, err := EncodeDriveLayoutEx(l)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	decoded, err := DecodeDriveLayoutEx(encoded)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if decoded.DiskGUID != l.DiskGUID {
		t.Errorf("DiskGUID = %s, want %s", decoded.DiskGUID, l.DiskGUID)
	}
	if len(decoded.Partitions) != len(l.Partitions) {
		t.Fatalf("Partitions = %d, want %d", len(decoded.Partitions), len(l.Partitions))
	}
	for i, want := range l.Partitions {
		got := decoded.Partitions[i]
		if got.Number != want.Number || got.TypeGUID != want.TypeGUID || got.PartGUID != want.PartGUID ||
			got.Name != want.Name || got.StartBytes != want.StartBytes || got.SizeBytes != want.SizeBytes || got.Attributes != want.Attributes {
			t.Errorf("partition %d = %+v, want %+v", i, got, want)
		}
	}
}

func TestEncodeDriveLayoutExByteOffsets(t *testing.T) {
	l := Layout{
		DiskGUID: "c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b",
		Partitions: []Partition{{
			Number:     7,
			TypeGUID:   "ebd0a0a2-b9e5-4433-87c0-68b6b72699c7",
			PartGUID:   "e3c9e316-0b5c-4db8-817d-f92df00215ae",
			Name:       "data",
			StartBytes: 0x1122334455,
			SizeBytes:  0x6677889900,
			Attributes: 0x8000000000000000,
		}},
	}
	b, err := EncodeDriveLayoutEx(l)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	const headerSize = 48
	const entrySize = 144
	if len(b) != headerSize+entrySize {
		t.Fatalf("len = %d, want %d", len(b), headerSize+entrySize)
	}
	// Header.
	if got := le32(b[0:4]); got != 1 { // PARTITION_STYLE_GPT
		t.Errorf("header PartitionStyle@0 = %d, want 1", got)
	}
	if got := le32(b[4:8]); got != 1 {
		t.Errorf("header PartitionCount@4 = %d, want 1", got)
	}
	diskGUID, _ := GUIDToBytes(l.DiskGUID)
	if !bytesEqual(b[8:24], diskGUID[:]) {
		t.Errorf("header GPT.DiskId@8 mismatch")
	}
	// Partition entry at offset 48.
	e := b[headerSize:]
	if got := le32(e[0:4]); got != 1 {
		t.Errorf("entry PartitionStyle@0 = %d, want 1", got)
	}
	if got := le64(e[8:16]); got != uint64(l.Partitions[0].StartBytes) {
		t.Errorf("entry StartingOffset@8 = %#x, want %#x", got, l.Partitions[0].StartBytes)
	}
	if got := le64(e[16:24]); got != uint64(l.Partitions[0].SizeBytes) {
		t.Errorf("entry PartitionLength@16 = %#x, want %#x", got, l.Partitions[0].SizeBytes)
	}
	if got := le32(e[24:28]); got != 7 {
		t.Errorf("entry PartitionNumber@24 = %d, want 7", got)
	}
	if e[28] != 1 { // RewritePartition must be TRUE on every entry of a SET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX, or Windows ignores the entry
		t.Errorf("entry RewritePartition@28 = %d, want 1", e[28])
	}
	if e[29] != 0 {
		t.Errorf("entry IsServicePartition@29 = %d, want 0", e[29])
	}
	typeGUID, _ := GUIDToBytes(l.Partitions[0].TypeGUID)
	if !bytesEqual(e[32:48], typeGUID[:]) {
		t.Errorf("entry GPT.PartitionType@32 mismatch")
	}
	partGUID, _ := GUIDToBytes(l.Partitions[0].PartGUID)
	if !bytesEqual(e[48:64], partGUID[:]) {
		t.Errorf("entry GPT.PartitionId@48 mismatch")
	}
	if got := le64(e[64:72]); got != l.Partitions[0].Attributes {
		t.Errorf("entry GPT.Attributes@64 = %#x, want %#x", got, l.Partitions[0].Attributes)
	}
	// Name@72 is UTF-16LE, 36 code units wide, NUL-padded.
	nameBytes := e[72:144]
	if len(nameBytes) != 72 { // 36 * 2
		t.Fatalf("Name field = %d bytes, want 72", len(nameBytes))
	}
}

// TestEncodeDriveLayoutExRejectsMissingPartGUID: Windows does NOT invent a
// PartitionId for a GPT entry whose GUID is zero — it writes the zero GUID.
// The encoder therefore refuses an empty PartGUID; the caller (winProvision,
// W06b Task 11) fills it with NewGUID() when the layout recorded none.
func TestEncodeDriveLayoutExRejectsMissingPartGUID(t *testing.T) {
	l := Layout{DiskGUID: "c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b", Partitions: []Partition{{
		Number: 1, TypeGUID: "ebd0a0a2-b9e5-4433-87c0-68b6b72699c7", SizeBytes: 100,
	}}}
	if _, err := EncodeDriveLayoutEx(l); err == nil {
		t.Error("expected an error for a partition with no PartGUID")
	}
}

// TestGUIDToBytesMixedEndianVector is the plan-wide GUID byte-order pin
// (also used for MountedDevices DMIO:ID values in W06c).
func TestGUIDToBytesMixedEndianVector(t *testing.T) {
	got, err := GUIDToBytes("{12345678-1234-5678-9abc-def012345678}")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	const want = "78563412341278569abcdef012345678"
	if hex.EncodeToString(got[:]) != want {
		t.Errorf("GUIDToBytes = %x, want %s", got, want)
	}
}

func TestNewGUIDIsLowerCaseV4(t *testing.T) {
	g, err := NewGUID()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if len(g) != 36 || g[14] != '4' || strings.ToLower(g) != g {
		t.Fatalf("NewGUID() = %q, want a lower-case version-4 GUID", g)
	}
	if _, err := GUIDToBytes(g); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("NewGUID() output does not round-trip: %v", err)
	}
}

// TestEncodeCreateDiskGPT pins CREATE_DISK (GPT variant) on x64:
// PartitionStyle u32@0, CREATE_DISK_GPT.DiskId GUID@4,
// CREATE_DISK_GPT.MaxPartitionCount u32@20, sizeof = 24.
func TestEncodeCreateDiskGPT(t *testing.T) {
	b, err := EncodeCreateDiskGPT("12345678-1234-5678-9abc-def012345678")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if len(b) != 24 {
		t.Fatalf("len = %d, want 24", len(b))
	}
	if le32(b[0:4]) != 1 {
		t.Errorf("PartitionStyle@0 = %d, want 1 (GPT)", le32(b[0:4]))
	}
	if hex.EncodeToString(b[4:20]) != "78563412341278569abcdef012345678" {
		t.Errorf("DiskId@4 = %x", b[4:20])
	}
	if le32(b[20:24]) != 128 {
		t.Errorf("MaxPartitionCount@20 = %d, want 128", le32(b[20:24]))
	}
}

// TestCopyUsableRange pins the header bytes WriteLayout carries over from
// the disk's current layout (StartingUsableOffset@24, UsableLength@32,
// MaxPartitionCount@40): Windows computes them at CREATE_DISK time and a
// SET_DRIVE_LAYOUT_EX that zeroes them is rejected with
// ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER on some builds.
func TestCopyUsableRange(t *testing.T) {
	dst := make([]byte, 48)
	cur := make([]byte, 48)
	for i := range cur {
		cur[i] = byte(i)
	}
	if err := CopyUsableRange(dst, cur); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for i := 0; i < 48; i++ {
		inRange := i >= 24 && i < 44
		if inRange && dst[i] != byte(i) {
			t.Errorf("byte %d = %d, want copied %d", i, dst[i], i)
		}
		if !inRange && dst[i] != 0 {
			t.Errorf("byte %d = %d, want untouched 0", i, dst[i])
		}
	}
	if err := CopyUsableRange(dst, make([]byte, 10)); err == nil {
		t.Error("expected an error for a truncated current layout")
	}
}

func TestEncodeDriveLayoutExRejectsNameOver36UTF16Units(t *testing.T) {
	name := ""
	for i := 0; i < 37; i++ {
		name += "x"
	}
	l := Layout{DiskGUID: "c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b", Partitions: []Partition{{
		Number: 1, TypeGUID: "ebd0a0a2-b9e5-4433-87c0-68b6b72699c7", PartGUID: "e3c9e316-0b5c-4db8-817d-f92df00215ae", Name: name, SizeBytes: 100,
	}}}
	if _, err := EncodeDriveLayoutEx(l); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "36 UTF-16") {
		t.Errorf("err = %v, want the 36-code-unit name error", err)
	}
}

func le32(b []byte) uint32 { return uint32(b[0]) | uint32(b[1])<<8 | uint32(b[2])<<16 | uint32(b[3])<<24 }
func le64(b []byte) uint64 {
	var v uint64
	for i := 7; i >= 0; i-- {
		v = v<<8 | uint64(b[i])
	}
	return v
}
func bytesEqual(a, b []byte) bool {
	if len(a) != len(b) {
		return false
	}
	for i := range a {
		if a[i] != b[i] {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}
```

```go
// TestAssess_WindowsSecondDiskIgnoredNotMultiDisk documents (Part 0 Global
// Constraints "Assess... treat systemMountPoints as Linux-only") that a
// Windows source's second data disk (e.g. D:\) is never flagged
// ReasonMultiDisk the way a Linux /var-on-a-second-disk is: systemMountPoints
// is a POSIX mount-point set ("/", "/boot", …) that a Windows drive letter
// like `D:\` can never match, so the multi-disk dependency check silently
// no-ops for Windows already — this test exists so that invariant can never
// regress silently if someone "fixes" systemMountPoints to also list drive
// letters, believing multi-disk detection is Windows-aware today when it is
// not, and is not meant to be (extra Windows data disks are file-backed up,
// not disk-rebuilt).
func TestAssess_WindowsSecondDiskIgnoredNotMultiDisk(t *testing.T) {
	m := &Manifest{
		SchemaVersion: SchemaVersion, Platform: "windows", BootMode: BootModeUEFI,
		Disks: []Disk{
			{Name: `\\.\PHYSICALDRIVE0`, TableType: "gpt", IsSystem: true, Partitions: []Partition{
				{Number: 1, TypeGUID: GUIDEFISystem, Filesystem: "fat32", Role: RoleEFI, Encryption: EncryptionNone},
				{Number: 2, TypeGUID: GUIDMicrosoftBasic, PartUUID: "aa", Filesystem: "ntfs", MountPoint: `C:\`, Role: RoleRoot, Encryption: EncryptionNone},
			}},
			{Name: `\\.\PHYSICALDRIVE1`, TableType: "gpt", Partitions: []Partition{
				{Number: 1, TypeGUID: GUIDMicrosoftBasic, PartUUID: "bb", Filesystem: "ntfs", MountPoint: `D:\`, Role: RoleData, Encryption: EncryptionNone},
			}},
		},
	}
	got := Assess(m)
	if !got.Restorable || len(got.Reasons) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("Assess() = %+v, want Restorable with a second data disk ignored", got)
	}
}

func containsReason(reasons []string, want string) bool {
	for _, r := range reasons {
		if r == want {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}
```

```go
func TestDiskNumberFromName(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name    string
		in      string
		want    int
		wantOK  bool
	}{
		{"disk 0", `\\.\PHYSICALDRIVE0`, 0, true},
		{"disk 12", `\\.\PHYSICALDRIVE12`, 12, true},
		{"not a physical drive name", `\\.\Volume{abc}`, 0, false},
		{"trailing garbage", `\\.\PHYSICALDRIVE1x`, 0, false},
		{"empty", "", 0, false},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got, ok := diskNumberFromName(tt.in)
			if ok != tt.wantOK || (ok && got != tt.want) {
				t.Errorf("diskNumberFromName(%q) = (%d, %v), want (%d, %v)", tt.in, got, ok, tt.want, tt.wantOK)
			}
		})
	}
}
```
