Note the payload type is `bareMetalRebuildPayload` (lowercase), `validate()` is a pointer-receiver method, and `execBareMetalRebuild` takes `(ctx, payload, rebuildFn)` — the new hyperv hook is a **separate** package var, `createRebuildVMFn`, not a reuse of `rebuildFn`:

```go
func TestValidate_RejectsHyperVOffWindows(t *testing.T) {
	if runtime.GOOS == "windows" {
		t.Skip("this refusal only fires on non-Windows hosts")
	}
	p := &bareMetalRebuildPayload{
		RecoveryID: "rec-1", Token: "brz_rec_test", Server: "https://breeze.example.com",
		Target: struct {
			Kind           string `json:"kind"`
			Path           string `json:"path"`
			ImageSizeBytes int64  `json:"imageSizeBytes,omitempty"`
		}{Kind: "vhdx", Path: "/var/tmp/x.vhdx"},
		HyperV: &hyperVPayload{VMName: "w06-proof"},
	}
	err := p.validate()
	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "hyperv is only supported on Windows hosts") {
		t.Fatalf("validate() = %v, want hyperv-not-windows error", err)
	}
}

func TestExecBareMetalRebuild_CreatesVMOnHyperVSuccess(t *testing.T) {
	origCreate := createRebuildVMFn
	defer func() { createRebuildVMFn = origCreate }()

	var gotVHDXPath string
	var gotReq hyperv.CreateVMRequest
	createRebuildVMFn = func(_ context.Context, req hyperv.CreateVMRequest) error {
		gotVHDXPath, gotReq = req.VHDXPath, req
		return nil
	}

	server, _ := newTokenModeTestServer(t, biosLayoutJSON(t))
	fake := &fakeRebuild{}
	fake.push(&rebuild.Result{Status: "completed", Target: rebuild.Target{Path: "/var/tmp/x.vhdx"}, Plan: &rebuild.Plan{}}, nil)

	payload := testBareMetalRebuildPayload(t, server.URL) // extend this helper's JSON with an hyperv block
	result := execBareMetalRebuild(context.Background(), payload, fake.fn)

	if !result.Success {
		t.Fatalf("result = %+v", result)
	}
	var res bareMetalRebuildResult
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(result.Stdout), &res); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if !res.VMCreated {
		t.Fatalf("res.VMCreated = false, want true")
	}
	if gotVHDXPath != res.Target.Path {
		t.Fatalf("createRebuildVMFn VHDXPath = %q, want %q", gotVHDXPath, res.Target.Path)
	}
	if gotReq.VMName != "w06-proof" {
		t.Fatalf("createRebuildVMFn req = %+v", gotReq)
	}
}

func TestExecBareMetalRebuild_VMCreateFailureKeepsCompletedResult(t *testing.T) {
	origCreate := createRebuildVMFn
	defer func() { createRebuildVMFn = origCreate }()
	createRebuildVMFn = func(context.Context, hyperv.CreateVMRequest) error {
		return errors.New("New-VM: access denied")
	}

	server, _ := newTokenModeTestServer(t, biosLayoutJSON(t))
	fake := &fakeRebuild{}
	fake.push(&rebuild.Result{Status: "completed", Target: rebuild.Target{Path: "/var/tmp/x.vhdx"}, Plan: &rebuild.Plan{}}, nil)

	payload := testBareMetalRebuildPayload(t, server.URL) // extend this helper's JSON with an hyperv block
	result := execBareMetalRebuild(context.Background(), payload, fake.fn)

	// The command itself must still report success — the rebuild succeeded;
	// only the convenience VM did not, and that failure must be visible
	// (e.g. as a Warning on the result) without masking the completed run.
	if !result.Success {
		t.Fatalf("expected the completed rebuild to still report success, got %+v", result)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(result.Stdout, "New-VM: access denied") {
		t.Fatalf("result.Stdout = %q, want it to record the VM-create failure", result.Stdout)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.**

`agent/cmd/breeze-backup/exec_bare_metal_rebuild.go` — the real payload/validate/exec shapes (confirmed by direct read) differ from an earlier draft: the payload type is `bareMetalRebuildPayload` (`:26-36`, pointer-receiver `validate()` at `:40-51`), and `execBareMetalRebuild` (`:75-115`) already threads `res` through a `bareMetalRebuildResult{*rebuild.Result; RecoveryID string}` wrapper and returns `backupipc.BackupCommandResult{Success, Stdout, Stderr}` (no `Error`/`Data` fields, no `mgr *backup.BackupManager` parameter). The VM-create hook goes right after `runTokenModeRebuild` returns, before the result is marshalled:

The real import block (`:3-14`, confirmed by direct read: `context, encoding/json, errors, fmt, log/slog, path/filepath, time, rebuild, backupipc`) has neither `runtime` nor the `hyperv` package — both are needed by the code below (`runtime.GOOS` in `validate()`, `hyperv.CreateVMRequest`/`hyperv.ErrHyperVNotSupported` in `execBareMetalRebuild`) and must be added:

```go
import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"log/slog"
	"path/filepath"
	"runtime" // new — p.validate()'s hyperv-requires-windows check
	"time"

	"github.com/breeze-rmm/agent/internal/backup/hyperv" // new — hyperv.CreateVMRequest
	"github.com/breeze-rmm/agent/internal/backup/rebuild"
	"github.com/breeze-rmm/agent/internal/backupipc"
)
```

```go
// hyperVPayload mirrors apps/api/src/services/bareMetalRebuildSchemas.ts's
// hypervOptionsSchema field-for-field.
type hyperVPayload struct {
	VMName     string `json:"vmName"`
	SwitchName string `json:"switchName,omitempty"`
	MemoryMB   int64  `json:"memoryMb,omitempty"`
	CPUCount   int    `json:"cpuCount,omitempty"`
}

// bareMetalRebuildPayload — existing fields at :26-36 unchanged; HyperV added.
type bareMetalRebuildPayload struct {
	// … existing RecoveryID, Token, Server, Target, Identity, OutputDir fields unchanged …
	HyperV *hyperVPayload `json:"hyperv,omitempty"`
}

// validate — existing checks at :40-51 (filepath.IsAbs on Target.Path) unchanged; this is appended.
func (p *bareMetalRebuildPayload) validate() error {
	// … existing switch over RecoveryID/Token/Server/Target.Kind/Target.Path, unchanged …
	if p.HyperV != nil && runtime.GOOS != "windows" {
		return fmt.Errorf("hyperv is only supported on Windows hosts")
	}
	if p.HyperV != nil && p.HyperV.VMName == "" {
		return fmt.Errorf("hyperv.vmName is required when hyperv is set")
	}
	return nil
}

// createRebuildVMFn is the hyperv VM-create seam; overridden in tests. Distinct
// from execBareMetalRebuild's own rebuildFn parameter, which is the rebuild
// ENGINE callback (rebuild.Run), not the VM-create step.
var createRebuildVMFn = createRebuildVM

// execBareMetalRebuild — unchanged through the runTokenModeRebuild call
// (:75-98); this replaces the tail (:99-115) that marshals and returns the result.
func execBareMetalRebuild(parentCtx context.Context, payload json.RawMessage, rebuildFn func(context.Context, rebuild.Options) (*rebuild.Result, error)) backupipc.BackupCommandResult {
	// … unmarshal, validate, ctx/cancel, target/opts/report build, runTokenModeRebuild call (:75-98) unchanged …

	if errors.Is(runErr, rebuild.ErrUnsupportedHost) {
		return fail(rebuild.ErrUnsupportedHost.Error())
	}
	if res == nil {
		if runErr == nil {
			runErr = errors.New("rebuild returned no result")
		}
		return fail(runErr.Error())
	}

	// New: create the convenience Hyper-V VM after a completed rebuild, if asked.
	var vmCreateWarning string
	if res.Status == "completed" && p.HyperV != nil {
		req := hyperv.CreateVMRequest{
			VMName:     p.HyperV.VMName,
			VHDXPath:   res.Target.Path,
			SwitchName: p.HyperV.SwitchName,
			MemoryMB:   p.HyperV.MemoryMB,
			CPUCount:   p.HyperV.CPUCount,
		}
		if err := createRebuildVMFn(ctx, req); err != nil {
			// The completed rebuild result MUST survive a VM-create failure —
			// the rebuild itself succeeded; only the convenience VM did not.
			// Recorded as a warning on the result rather than failing the command.
			vmCreateWarning = "hyperv VM creation failed: " + err.Error()
			res.Warnings = append(res.Warnings, vmCreateWarning)
		} else {
			res.VMCreated = true
		}
	}

	body, merr := json.Marshal(bareMetalRebuildResult{Result: res, RecoveryID: p.RecoveryID})
	if merr != nil {
		return fail(fmt.Sprintf("failed to marshal result: %v", merr))
	}
	if runErr != nil && res.Status != "refused" {
		reason := runErr.Error()
		if res.Error != "" {
			reason = res.Error
		}
		return backupipc.BackupCommandResult{Success: false, Stdout: string(body), Stderr: reason}
	}
	return ok(string(body))
}
```

`agent/internal/backup/hyperv/vmcreate.go` (new, `//go:build windows` — bare filename per this package's real stub convention; reuses `runPS`/`escapePSString` from `discovery.go:198,208`, both already `package hyperv`):

```go
//go:build windows

package hyperv

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
)

// CreateVMRequest is the input to CreateVMFromVHDX. It intentionally has NO
// default-switch fallback (unlike createAndConfigureVM in vmrestore.go): a
// same-host VM created from an original-identity rebuild image must not get
// a NIC unless the caller explicitly names a switch — giving it one by
// default would let the VM renew the still-live source machine's Kerberos
// computer-account password out from under it.
type CreateVMRequest struct {
	VMName     string
	VHDXPath   string
	SwitchName string // "" → no network adapter
	MemoryMB   int64  // 0 → 4096 default
	CPUCount   int    // 0 → 2 default
}

// CreateVMFromVHDX creates a Generation 2 VM booting the given VHDX, with
// Secure Boot enabled against the Microsoft Windows certificate template,
// and no network adapter unless req.SwitchName is set. Uses the package's
// existing runPS (discovery.go:198, no context deadline of its own — matches
// every other PowerShell call in this package, e.g. createAndConfigureVM in
// vmrestore.go) rather than inventing a new context-bounded variant.
func CreateVMFromVHDX(ctx context.Context, req CreateVMRequest) error {
	_ = ctx // reserved for a future ctx-aware runPS variant; not used today — see note above
	memoryMB := req.MemoryMB
	if memoryMB == 0 {
		memoryMB = 4096
	}
	cpuCount := req.CPUCount
	if cpuCount == 0 {
		cpuCount = 2
	}

	vmNameEsc := escapePSString(req.VMName)
	vhdxPathEsc := escapePSString(req.VHDXPath)
	memBytes := memoryMB * 1024 * 1024

	createCmd := fmt.Sprintf(
		`New-VM -Name '%s' -Generation 2 -MemoryStartupBytes %d -VHDPath '%s'`,
		vmNameEsc, memBytes, vhdxPathEsc,
	)
	if _, err := runPS(createCmd); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("New-VM: %w", err)
	}

	cpuCmd := fmt.Sprintf(`Set-VM -Name '%s' -ProcessorCount %d`, vmNameEsc, cpuCount)
	if _, err := runPS(cpuCmd); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("Set-VM -ProcessorCount: %w", err)
	}

	firmwareCmd := fmt.Sprintf(
		`Set-VMFirmware -VMName '%s' -EnableSecureBoot On -SecureBootTemplate MicrosoftWindows`,
		vmNameEsc,
	)
	if _, err := runPS(firmwareCmd); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("Set-VMFirmware: %w", err)
	}

	if req.SwitchName == "" {
		removeNicCmd := fmt.Sprintf(`Remove-VMNetworkAdapter -VMName '%s'`, vmNameEsc)
		if _, err := runPS(removeNicCmd); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("Remove-VMNetworkAdapter: %w", err)
		}
		return nil
	}

	connectCmd := fmt.Sprintf(
		`Connect-VMNetworkAdapter -VMName '%s' -SwitchName '%s'`,
		vmNameEsc, escapePSString(req.SwitchName),
	)
	if _, err := runPS(connectCmd); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("Connect-VMNetworkAdapter: %w", err)
	}
	return nil
}
```

`apps/web/src/components/backup/VMRestoreWizard.tsx` (real anchors, confirmed by direct read: `Snapshot.layoutManifestKey :41`, `isAbsoluteVhdxPath :55-58`, target host picker `osType:'windows' :87`, rebuild host picker `osType:'linux' :95`, `rebuildEngineAvailable :158`, `canSubmit :165-168`):

```tsx
interface Snapshot {
  id: string;
  layoutManifestKey: string;
  layoutPlatform?: 'linux' | 'windows' | null; // new — drives the rebuild host OS filter default
  // … existing fields unchanged …
}

/** isAbsoluteVhdxPath mirrors apps/api's isAbsoluteRebuildPath (not imported
 * — the web bundle never imports server code): a POSIX absolute path or a
 * Windows drive-letter absolute path, UNC explicitly rejected. */
function isAbsoluteVhdxPath(p: string): boolean {
  if (p.startsWith('\\\\')) return false;
  return /^[A-Za-z]:\\/.test(p) || p.startsWith('/');
}

// … target host picker (osType:'windows') unchanged …

// rebuild host picker — was a fixed osType:'linux' filter; now derived per snapshot
const rebuildHostOsFilter = selectedSnapshot?.layoutPlatform ?? 'linux';
const rebuildHosts = hosts.filter((h) => h.osType === rebuildHostOsFilter);

// … inside the Mode step, after the rebuild host <select> …
{selectedRebuildHost?.osType === 'windows' && (
  <fieldset>
    <legend>{t('backup:hypervOptional')}</legend>
    <label htmlFor="hyperv-vm-name">{t('backup:hypervVmName')}</label>
    <input
      id="hyperv-vm-name"
      value={hypervVmName}
      onChange={(e) => setHypervVmName(e.target.value)}
      placeholder={t('backup:hypervVmNamePlaceholder')}
    />
    <label htmlFor="hyperv-switch">{t('backup:hypervSwitch')}</label>
    <input
      id="hyperv-switch"
      value={hypervSwitchName}
      onChange={(e) => setHypervSwitchName(e.target.value)}
      placeholder={t('backup:hypervSwitchPlaceholder')}
    />
    {!hypervSwitchName && <p className="hint">{t('backup:hypervNoNicHint')}</p>}
  </fieldset>
)}
```

Replace the live-registry paragraph at `:178-183` with the offline-apply description, sourced from Part 0's "Hives" Global Constraint:

```mdx
### How Windows system state is restored

Breeze never restores a Windows machine's registry by writing into a running
system's `HKLM` — that would corrupt whatever is currently running the
recovery. Instead, each hive (`SYSTEM`, `SOFTWARE`, `SAM`, `SECURITY`,
`DEFAULT`, plus every user's `NTUSER.DAT`) is loaded **offline**, under a
temporary registry key, directly from the files inside the backup — never
via `reg restore`, `reg import`, or `bcdedit /import`. If any of the four
machine hives is missing from the restored file tree, all four are replaced
together from a second, independently captured copy rather than mixing
sources (mixing would split a machine's password secrets from the rest of
its identity). Every hive is unloaded again before the machine boots.
```

Update the Linux-only language at `:213-215` (the `<Aside type="note" title="Scope">` on `breeze-backup rebuild`, which currently reads "Linux hosts and Linux snapshots only, run from a booted Linux environment; the snapshot must be marked **Bare-metal restorable** and the target must be UEFI/GPT."):

```mdx
<Aside type="note" title="Scope">
  Run from a booted host matching the snapshot's platform — a Linux snapshot
  rebuilds on Linux, a Windows snapshot on Windows (Breeze checks this and
  refuses the pair otherwise); the snapshot must be marked **Bare-metal
  restorable** and the target must be UEFI/GPT. The [recovery media](#recover-from-breeze-recovery-media)
  runs it for you; the command below is for scripted or unusual recoveries
  and does not appear in the device's command history. [Restore as VM](#restore-as-vm)
  and [DR bare-metal rebuild steps](/backup/disaster-recovery/#bare-metal-rebuild-step)
  also run this engine, targeting a VHDX image instead of a disk.
</Aside>
```

And at `:254-268` (the real `## Restore as VM` section) — its title reference to "Rebuild engine (Linux)" and its closing Aside both need the platform-neutral/VM-creation update, matching the i18n change in Task 22:

```mdx
The **VM Restore Wizard**'s **Rebuild engine** option runs the same [rebuild engine](#rebuilding-a-linux-disk-from-scratch-breeze-backup-rebuild)
described above, but writes the result straight to a **VHDX** disk image
instead of a physical disk on a platform-matched rebuild host, then boots it
as a Hyper-V VM when the target is Windows — a Linux target still writes the
VHDX for you to attach yourself. It's offered alongside **Full Restore** and
**Instant Boot** whenever the selected snapshot carries a layout manifest
(the same **Bare-metal restorable** snapshots the recovery media uses).

<Steps>
1. Open the restore workflow for the device and choose **Restore as VM**.
2. Select the backup snapshot to restore.
3. On the **Restore mode** step, choose **Rebuild engine**.
4. Pick a **rebuild host** running the same platform as the snapshot — an
   online device running the Breeze helper (Linux: `qemu-utils` installed;
   Windows: the Hyper-V role) with enough free disk space for the image.
   Preflight refuses the run if either requirement isn't met.
5. Enter an absolute **output path** on the rebuild host ending in `.vhdx`
   (a POSIX path on Linux, a drive-letter path on Windows).
6. On a Windows rebuild host, optionally name a Hyper-V VM and virtual
   switch — Breeze creates the VM from the finished VHDX automatically, with
   no network adapter unless you name a switch.
7. Click **Start Rebuild**.
</Steps>

The rebuilt image always gets a **new machine identity** — a new machine ID,
a `-restored` hostname, and no agent enrollment — since it's building a
standalone copy, not reclaiming the original device.
```

(This replaces the section's closing `<Aside type="note">Breeze does not yet create the Hyper-V VM from the VHDX automatically...</Aside>` entirely — leaving that sentence in place after Task 21 ships would actively contradict the shipped feature.)

New "Windows fidelity" section:

```mdx
## Windows fidelity

What a Windows whole-machine restore preserves, and what it doesn't yet:

**Preserved:**
- NTFS security descriptors (owner, group, DACL) for every file and
  directory, so a rebuilt `C:\Windows\System32\config` keeps its original
  ACLs rather than inheriting Authenticated-Users-Modify from a fresh
  volume — this closes the class of issue behind CVE-2021-36934.
- Partition GUIDs, the EFI boot configuration, and (best-effort) boot-start
  storage driver settings for the disk controller the target machine
  actually has.
- The machine's SID is never regenerated.

**Not yet restored:**
- Hard links, alternate data streams, and EFS-encrypted file contents.
- Windows Recovery Environment (WinRE) partition contents — the partition
  is recreated and formatted but left empty; `reagentc` is not run.
- A BitLocker-protected source volume restores in **plaintext**. Breeze
  records the intent to re-encrypt on first boot; the executor for that
  intent ships with the Windows recovery media (W07).
- A domain controller's system state is refused outright — recovering a DC
  this way risks a broken AD replication topology; use your normal DC
  recovery procedure instead.
```

`apps/docs/src/content/docs/backup/disaster-recovery.mdx` (around lines 57-65 in the target state) — rebuild host requirements per OS:

```mdx
### Rebuild host requirements

| | Linux rebuild host | Windows rebuild host |
|---|---|---|
| OS | Same distro family is not required; any Linux with the tools below | Windows Server or Windows 10/11 with Hyper-V |
| Tools | `util-linux`, `sgdisk`, `dosfstools`, `e2fsprogs`/`xfsprogs`, `grub-efi`, `efibootmgr`, `qemu-utils` (Linux only — used to convert a raw image to VHDX; not needed on Windows, which writes VHDX natively) | Built-in `bcdboot.exe`, `dism.exe`, `format.com`; Hyper-V role for Restore-as-VM |
| Target snapshot platform | Must match the rebuild host's OS — Breeze refuses the pair otherwise | Must match the rebuild host's OS — Breeze refuses the pair otherwise |
```

`docs/superpowers/specs/backup/2026-09-10-bare-metal-boot-media-recovery-design.md` — ALSO change the frontmatter `supersedes:` line (`:6`), which today points at a non-existent `§6.4`, to read `see §6.1` so the reference finally resolves; then the new `### 6.1 Windows offline state apply` (not `6.4` — confirmed by direct read that §6, `:71-86`, is a flat 7-item numbered phase list with no existing subsections, unlike §5's real `5.1`–`5.4`; this is the first subsection under §6, inserted after the "Targets:" paragraph and before `## 7. Boot media and console`):

```markdown
### 6.1 Windows offline state apply

Registry hives are the authoritative source when present in the VSS file
tree (`Windows\System32\config\{SYSTEM,SOFTWARE,SAM,SECURITY,DEFAULT}` plus
each `.LOG1`/`.LOG2`, and every `Users\*\NTUSER.DAT`). If any one of
SYSTEM/SOFTWARE/SAM/SECURITY is missing from the tree, all four are
replaced together from the independently captured system-state artifacts
(`system-state/registry/<HIVE>`) and their `.LOG1`/`.LOG2` files are
deleted — mixing a tree hive with an artifact hive from a different capture
point would split LSA secrets from the machine password. If an artifact is
missing too, the restore fails naming the hive.

Every hive is mounted at a run-scoped key (`HKLM\BRZ_<runid>_<HIVE>`),
never the live `HKLM`. Edits target `ControlSet00<Select\Default>`; when
`Select\Current` differs from `Select\Default`, the same edits are applied
to both. `MountedDevices` is always rewritten regardless of whether the
disk layout changed: `\DosDevices\C:` is set to the restored root
partition's GUID (`DMIO:ID:` + the 16-byte mixed-endian GUID exactly as
`IOCTL_DISK_GET_PARTITION_INFO_EX` returns it), and every other
`\DosDevices\<letter>:` value whose GUID is not on the rebuilt disk is
deleted; `\??\Volume{…}` values are left untouched.

`bcdboot <root>\Windows /s <ESP letter>: /f UEFI /v` regenerates the EFI
system partition from the restored tree's own `bcdboot.exe` when present,
falling back to the host's with a warning. For a VHDX target on a live
host, `/p` is added to preserve the host's own UEFI firmware boot entries
— proven unchanged before/after by the lab run in §11. The captured BCD
store (`system-state/boot/bcd_export`) is never imported.

A BitLocker-protected source restores in plaintext. The phase writes a
post-restore-actions intent file rather than re-encrypting immediately —
re-encryption executes from the restored agent at first boot with a NEW
recovery password once W07 ships the executor; the escrowed key is never
carried on recovery media.
```

Record the deviations in §12 — confirmed by direct read, §12 (`:163-165`) is a **single prose paragraph** ("Block-level imaging; LVM, LUKS, RAID, BIOS/MBR, multi-disk targets (refused, not silently attempted); macOS bare-metal; ..."), not a table, so this is two new bullet items appended below that paragraph, not table rows:

```markdown
- **D-BL** — BitLocker re-encryption is recorded as intent in this wave, not
  executed; the executor ships with W07 because it needs a booted disk
  target to test against, and disk targets aren't shippable until WinPE
  media exists.
- **D-ACL** — NTFS security descriptors are captured and restored in this
  wave, ahead of where the original spec sequenced them, because a rebuilt
  `C:\Windows\System32\config` without them is a HiveNightmare-class
  vulnerability (CVE-2021-36934) the day it boots.
```

`docs/superpowers/plans/backup/2026-09-10-bmr-windows-offline-hive-decision.md` — **the real document's "Option B" (confirmed by direct read, `§3` and `§4`) is not "apply hives offline" — it is "drop live hive/BCD apply from the reinstall-then-recover path entirely, keep only files + certs + firewall auto-applied."** That decision is about `agent/internal/backup/bmr/restore_windows.go`'s `RestoreSystemState`, the **reinstall-then-recover** code path (`bmr-recover`), which edits an already-booted target OS's own live hives and therefore genuinely cannot go offline without a WinPE-class boot environment (Option A, rejected as multi-quarter infrastructure this wave doesn't build). Wave 6's offline hive apply is a **different code path**: the rebuild engine (`agent/internal/backup/rebuild`) provisions a brand-new, never-yet-booted target disk/VHDX from scratch, so "offline" is not an added capability there — the target was never a running OS to begin with, so `reg load`/offline hive editing was always available on that path without needing WinPE at all. The two paths reached different conclusions for a reason, not by inconsistency; the update below says so explicitly rather than claiming Wave 6 "implements Option B":

```markdown
## 2026-09-22 update

Wave 6 does not change this decision or its scope — `bmr-recover`
(reinstall-then-recover, `restore_windows.go`) still ships Option B exactly
as decided here: hives/BCD stay collected-for-reference only, never
live-applied. Wave 6 instead adds offline hive apply to a **separate** code
path, the rebuild engine (`agent/internal/backup/rebuild`, `breeze-backup
rebuild` / Restore-as-VM / DR bare-metal rebuild), which provisions an
unbooted target disk from scratch. That path was never subject to the
"live hive" constraint this document analyzes — there is no running target
OS to corrupt — so it was never an Option A vs. B choice for the rebuild
engine, and this decision stands unmodified for `bmr-recover`. The
rebuild engine's offline-apply mechanics (file-tree-first, all-four-hive
fallback, run-scoped `HKLM\BRZ_<runid>_<HIVE>` mount naming) are recorded in
spec §6.1, not here. `MountedDevices` rewriting was added there as a
corollary this wave discovered was necessary (not anticipated when this
document was written): restoring identical partition GUIDs is not
sufficient on its own when a target disk assigns them a different drive
letter than the source disk did.
```

- [ ] **Step 1: File follow-up issues.** Exact titles + bodies:

**(a) Title:** `Bare-metal capture: export driver packages for dissimilar-hardware Windows rebuilds`
**Body:**
> Wave 6 injects operator-supplied driver packages via `DISM /Add-Driver` (`Options.DriverDirs`), but nothing captures them from the source machine. Add a capture-side step using `pnputil /export-driver * <dir>` so a Windows whole-machine backup carries a driver package set the rebuild phase can use automatically for dissimilar-hardware restores, instead of requiring an operator to supply `--drivers` by hand. Today `driverquery` output is inventory-only (see `system-state/drivers/inventory.csv`) and cannot be replayed by DISM.
> Follow-up from Wave 6 (#5499), Part D Task 24, deliberate non-goal "No captured driver packages."

**(b) Title:** `Restore-as-VM: Linux guests on Windows hosts, cross-host New-VM, Instant Boot for Linux guests`
**Body:**
> Wave 5 promised same-platform Restore-as-VM (a Linux snapshot rebuilds and boots on a Linux rebuild host; a Windows snapshot on a Windows rebuild host via Hyper-V, shipped in Wave 6). Cross-platform and cross-host scenarios remain out of scope: booting a Linux guest under Hyper-V, creating a VM on a host other than the one that ran the rebuild engine, and Instant Boot for Linux guests (mirroring the existing Windows Instant Boot path in `agent/internal/backup/hyperv/instantboot.go`). File this as its own wave once there's demand; it needs its own design pass (Hyper-V has no native Linux Secure Boot template equivalent, and cross-host requires either a copy step or a shared-storage design).
> Follow-up from Wave 6 (#5499), deliberate non-goal "Linux guests on Windows hosts, cross-host New-VM, Instant Boot for Linux guests stay deferred (W05's promise)."

**(c) Title:** `Windows file backup: BackupRead/BackupWrite stream capture for hard links, ADS, and EFS`
**Body:**
> Wave 6a captures and restores NTFS security descriptors, closing the ACL gap (D-ACL). Hard links, alternate data streams, and EFS-encrypted file contents are still out of scope for the whole-machine Windows walker (`agent/internal/backup/backup.go`), which walks the file tree with ordinary `os.Open`/`os.Stat`-shaped calls. Restoring full NTFS fidelity for these needs `BackupRead`/`BackupWrite` stream-based capture and restore, which changes the on-disk backup format (streams, not just file bytes) — a real design task, not a small patch.
> Follow-up from Wave 6 (#5499), deliberate non-goal "Hard links, ADS, EFS, creation time and junction reparse data stay out of scope."

**(d) Title:** `Windows whole-machine restore: re-stage WinRE (Winre.wim) on the Recovery partition`
**Body:**
> The Recovery partition is recreated and correctly sized/formatted by Wave 6's `winProvision`/`winBoot` phases, but is left empty — `Winre.wim` is not part of the backup and `reagentc` is never run, so a restored machine has no working Windows RE (no in-place repair, no BitLocker recovery console on the restored machine, etc). Capturing and restoring `Winre.wim` plus re-running `reagentc /enable` against the rebuilt tree closes this gap.
> Follow-up from Wave 6 (#5499), deliberate non-goal "WinRE contents are not restored."

**(e) Title:** `Bare-metal recovery: raise or make configurable the 4h helper rebuild timeout for large Windows file counts`
**Body:**
> `bareMetalRebuildTimeout` in `agent/cmd/breeze-backup/exec_bare_metal_rebuild.go` is a fixed 4 hours. #6664 already tracks this as a general risk for large file counts; Wave 6's own lab proof puts real numbers against a Windows system disk specifically — see the W06-vhdx-restore-as-vm ledger row in `docs/testing/backup-assurance/2026-09-09-backup-assurance-campaign.md` §11.1 for the actual file count and elapsed restore-phase time from a ~130k+-file Windows `C:\` once that run has been executed and the ledger filled in. Cross-reference #6664 rather than duplicating it; this issue exists to attach the Windows-specific data point once measured.
> Follow-up from Wave 6 (#5499), Part D Task 24 step (e); references #6664.

- [ ] **Step 2: Lab proof on KIT.** This is an orchestrator runbook, not code — no files are created by this step beyond the ledger rows in Step "Pre-drafted ledger" below and whatever raw evidence lands under `scratchpad/` per this repo's existing lab convention (see the W03/W05/W09 rows above for the pattern: JSON job/recovery dumps, `.log` files, screenshots).

  1. Bring up a lab stack via the `worktree-stack` recipe against this branch's HEAD, with MinIO as the backup provider (same pattern as every prior wave's lab proof in the campaign ledger).
  2. Build the agent and helper from this branch's head and deliver them to KIT with `make dev-push` (swap KIT's production agent for the lab build for the duration of the proof; restore it afterward — same discipline as every prior KIT proof in the ledger, e.g. "H0 Hyper-V discovery").
  3. On WIN-A (the Server 2022 Gen2 UEFI guest on KIT), run a whole-machine `system_image` backup. Confirm the resulting snapshot reports `bareMetalRestorable: true` and that the manifest's deduplicated security-descriptor table is populated (`securityDescriptors` non-empty — this is the W06a fidelity work; without it the restore would silently come back with today's ACL behavior, not a test failure, so this check must be explicit).
  4. `POST /backup/restore/as-vm` with `engine: 'rebuild'`, the WIN-A snapshot, KIT as the rebuild host, and `hyperv: { vmName: 'w06-proof', switchName: <the lab's actual Hyper-V switch name> }`.
  5. Watch the recovery's state machine: expect `created → planned → restoring → validated → completed`, matching the shape every prior rebuild-engine lab row in the ledger already exercises.
  6. On completion, assert against the recovery's `result` JSON: `result.platform === 'windows'`, `result.vmCreated === true`.
  7. Connect to the new VM's console on KIT and confirm it boots to the Windows logon screen.
  8. Confirm the guest's hostname follows the `<BASE>-RESTORED` truncation rule (Part 0 Global Constraints "Identity") — expect something in the shape `WIN-A-RESTORED` or its 15-character-truncated equivalent if `identity: new` was used for this run; record whichever identity mode was actually requested and the resulting name.
  9. From an elevated PowerShell session inside the guest (or the same session via Hyper-V Guest Services if available): `icacls C:\Windows\System32\config` — expect only TrustedInstaller, SYSTEM, and Administrators listed, never Authenticated Users/Everyone (this is the D-ACL proof — a failure here means the security-descriptor restore silently regressed).
  10. `icacls C:\Users\<the seeded user>` — expect access restricted to that user (plus the standard SYSTEM/Administrators entries), not world-readable.
  11. On KIT (the host, not the guest): `bcdedit /enum firmware` before step 4 and again after step 7 completes — the two outputs must be identical, proving `bcdboot`'s `/p` flag preserved the host's own UEFI firmware boot entries rather than disturbing them (Part 0 Global Constraints "ESP and boot").
  12. `Get-VHD` on the created VHDX — record its reported size (both virtual and actual-on-disk) and every phase's timing from the recovery's `PhaseResult` list, for the ledger row below.
  13. Repeat the same proof as a DR rehearsal: create a DR plan targeting the WIN-A device with `BARE_METAL_REBUILD`, KIT as the rebuild host, and execute a rehearsal. Confirm the rehearsal forces `identity: new` (Part 0 Spec §8.3) rather than reusing WIN-A's own identity, and that the DR execution reaches `completed` from the recovery row (matching the reconciliation pattern already proven by the W05-dr-rehearsal/W09-dr-rehearsal-incremental rows in the ledger).
  14. Tear the lab stack down and restore KIT's production agent.

  **Explicit gap this proof does NOT close:** D6 (forcing boot-critical storage drivers to boot-start) is not meaningfully exercised by this run — WIN-A and KIT both use `storvsc` (Hyper-V's synthetic storage controller), so the offline SYSTEM edit either finds `storvsc` already boot-start or makes a change that a Gen2→Gen2 boot can't distinguish from a no-op. A real proof of D6 needs a source captured on a *different* storage controller (e.g. a physical machine with `storahci`/`stornvme`) rebuilt onto KIT's `storvsc`-based Hyper-V — out of scope for this wave's lab pass; note it as an open gap in the PR rather than claiming it proven.

  Pre-drafted §11.1 ledger rows (5-column format — `Cell | Rig | Build | Result | Evidence`, matching this file's existing header exactly):

  ```markdown
  | W06-dr-rehearsal-windows | DR plan targeting WIN-A, rebuild host KIT, rehearsal | same | (fill from the run — do not commit placeholder numbers) | (fill from the run — do not commit placeholder numbers) |

  | W06-host-nvram-unchanged | KIT `bcdedit /enum firmware` before/after the rebuild | same | (fill from the run — do not commit placeholder numbers) | (fill from the run — do not commit placeholder numbers) |
  ```

```
Closes #5499

Windows whole-machine recovery: platform-matched rebuild hosts, drive-letter
absolute paths across the rebuild command/DR/VM-restore schemas, per-host-OS
DR output directories, an optional same-host Hyper-V VM creation step after
a Windows rebuild (no network adapter unless a switch is named), the

snapshot platform, i18n across all locales, docs, five follow-up issues for
what's deliberately out of scope, and a proof run on the KIT lab (WIN-A

core, W06c OS state, W06d this PR). Depends on all three merging first.

One independent review round is expected before merge, per this repo's
"at most one independent code-review round" default; a fix that itself
touches tenancy/RLS, auth, migrations, or agent-shipped code would earn a
second round, but nothing in this PR does (the new migration is additive
and idempotent; no new tenancy shape).

This PR must be enqueued through normal CI (`gh pr merge --squash`), not
admin-bypassed — the campaign narrative in this repo's CLAUDE.md is explicit
that integration tests and the export-policy suites only run in the
Integration Tests job, and a stale-base or stacked-branch PR can read green
on `gh pr checks` while skipping them entirely. Dispatch CI per branch
(`gh workflow run CI --ref <branch>`) before relying on a green check here,
since W06a/b/c land on branches this PR is stacked on top of.
```
